## Blood Group Antigens

### ABO blood group antigens

**Term UUID:** `GSD:f054a590-0d24-5438-b871-64b001b53954`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- ABO glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- ABO(H) blood group glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- ABO blood group antigens are terminal glycan structures found on glycoproteins and glycolipids that define the A, B, and H blood group types. These glycans serve as receptors for certain viruses and are specifically recognized by lectins and antibodies, playing a key role in blood group determination and immune recognition. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch20/#:~:text=Free%20glycans%20%28e.g.%2C%20milk,that%20use%20ABO%20glycans%20as%20receptors (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch36/#:~:text=Thus%2C%20other%20sugar%20units%20linked%20to,diversity%20of%20recognition (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### blood group A determinant

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000029` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00066MO`

![blood group A determinant](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00066MO)

- **Image URL:** [G00066MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00066MO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00066MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:7ec6f782-4bc2-57ae-a15d-27c73f9a14f7`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- A antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- A blood group determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- blood group A glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- blood group A trisaccharide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A branched amino trisaccharide consisting of β-D-galactose having an α-L-fucosyl residue at the 2-position and an N-acetyl-α-D-galactosaminyl residue at the 3-position.[CHEBI:61012] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- The blood group A determinant is a glycan structure found on the surface of red blood cells that defines blood group A. It is formed by the terminal addition of N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) to the H antigen, a reaction catalyzed by A-transferase. This structure is a key component in distinguishing blood group A from other blood groups. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GalNAc(a1-3)[Fuc(a1-2)]Gal(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=O%20alleles%20at%20the%20ABO%20locus%20encode,of%20the%20A%20or%20B%20blood%20group%20antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=GMI-1070%2C%20targeting%20E-%2C%20P-%2C%20and,into%20%E2%80%9Cuniversal%20donor%E2%80%9D%20O%20red%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=GMI-1070%2C%20targeting%20E-%2C%20P-%2C%20and,into%20%E2%80%9Cuniversal%20donor%E2%80%9D%20O%20red%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- A readily detectable carbohydrate in the urine of two siblings with neuronal ceroid lipofuscinosis was found to be the blood group A trisaccharide.[PMID:7249368] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 17 references

- A branched amino tetrasaccharide comprising N-acetyl-β-D-glucosamine at the reducing end with a N-acetyl-α-D-galactosaminyl-(1→3)-[α-L-fucosyl-(1→2)]-β-D-galactosyl moiety attached at the 4-position.[CHEBI:62486] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- One hybridoma antibody (AH16), obtained on immunization with human gastric cancer cell MKN45, was directed to the blood group A determinant carried by both type 1 and type 2 chains. [PMID:6583282] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### blood group antigens

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b584b143-e0c4-5b8f-8523-f8b53ea2cfbe`

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A class of glycan epitopes found on glycoproteins and glycolipids that determine human blood groups, including the A, B, O(H), and other antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=coli.%20LPS%20consists%20of%20a%20base%20lipid,production%20in%20bacteria (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### blood group B determinant

**Term UUID:** `GSD:79a67483-e981-5415-9ec5-1725243f3795`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- B antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- B blood group determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- blood group B glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- The blood group B determinant is a specific glycan epitope, typically Galα1-3(Fucα1-2)Gal-R, present on the surface of red blood cells, glycoproteins, and glycolipids. It defines the blood group B antigen in humans and is formed by the terminal addition of galactose to the H antigen, catalyzed by B-transferase. This structure is recognized by specific antibodies and lectins, and is a key determinant in blood group typing. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=GMI-1070%2C%20targeting%20E-%2C%20P-%2C%20and,into%20%E2%80%9Cuniversal%20donor%E2%80%9D%20O%20red%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=GMI-1070%2C%20targeting%20E-%2C%20P-%2C%20and,into%20%E2%80%9Cuniversal%20donor%E2%80%9D%20O%20red%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch36/#:~:text=Galectin-1%20directly%20binds%20to,immune%20responses%20against%20pathogens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Similarly%2C%20blood%20group%20B,death%20from%20circulatory%20collapse (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch36/#:~:text=Galectins%20can%20also%20bind%20to,in%20the%20cytoplasm%20before%20secretion (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### blood group B type 1

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000033` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G31734OS`

![blood group B type 1](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G31734OS)

- **Image URL:** [G31734OS](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G31734OS)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G31734OS)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:0cd556eb-645e-4998-8348-4c3c3149852b`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- blood group B type 1 tetrasaccharide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- α-D-Galp-(1→3)-[α-L-Fucp-(1→2)]-β-D-Galp-(1→3)-β-D-GlcpNAc with β configuration at the anomeric carbon of the GlcNAc residue at the reducing end.[CHEBI:63242] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- The minimal binding epitope was identified as the blood group H type 1 determinant (Fucα2Galβ3GlcNAc), while an optimal binding epitope was created by addition of the terminal α3-linked galactose or N-acetylgalactosamine of the blood group B type 1 determinant (Galα3(Fucα2)Galβ3GlcNAc) and the blood group A type 1 determinant (GalNAcα3(Fucα2)-Galβ3GlcNAc). [PMID: 19208633] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

- blood group H glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- The blood group H determinant is a glycan antigen and epitope characterized by the presence of an α1-2-linked fucose residue, synthesized by α1-2 fucosyltransferase (FucT). It is formed by the addition of α1-2-linked fucose to the terminal galactose of a type 1 unit (Galβ1-3GlcNAc), resulting in the structure Fucα1-2Galβ1-3GlcNAc. This determinant serves as a precursor for ABO blood group antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Fuc(a1-2)Gal(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=For%20the%20UDP-Gal%20transporter%20from,Lex%20and%20SLex%20%28for%20the%20%CE%B11-3%20FucT%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=The%20Lewis%20b%20antigen%20%28Leb%29%20is,same%20epithelia%20that%20express%20FUT2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### P blood group antigens

**Term UUID:** `GSD:24ed65cb-f624-5126-8f05-af4d87063b0f`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- P-blood group (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- P blood group antigens are glycan structures, specifically glycosphingolipids, present on red blood cells. They serve as targets for specific antibodies, influencing blood group compatibility, and also act as receptors for certain bacterial adhesins, playing a role in susceptibility to urinary tract infections. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch46/#:~:text=Patients%20with%20the%20rare%20disorder,occurs%20after%20warming%20to%2037%C2%B0C (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch42/#:~:text=The%20structural%20proteins%20for%20pilus,in%20the%20%E2%80%9Cwhooping%20cough%E2%80%9D%20disease (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### core 1 O-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000186` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00031MO`

![core 1 O-glycan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00031MO)

- **Image URL:** [G00031MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00031MO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00031MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d30c0796-5386-5d5a-b374-96d79431db98`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- C1G (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Galβ1-3GalNAcαS/T (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- core 1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- core 1 O-GalNAc glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- The core 1 O-glycan, also known as Galβ1-3GalNAcα1-O-Ser/Thr, is a disaccharide structure that forms the basic core of mucin-type O-glycans in vertebrates and some invertebrates. It consists of a galactose β1-3 linked to N-acetylgalactosamine α-linked to serine or threonine residues on proteins. This structure is the most common O-GalNAc glycan, generated by the enzyme C1GALT1 (T-synthase), and serves as a precursor for further elongation and modification, including sialylation and sulfation, in O-glycosylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(a1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=In%20some%20cases%2C%20the%20length%20of%20the,glycolipids%20and%20glycosaminoglycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=elegans%20is%20similar%20to%20the%20chains,%28GSLs%29%20%28Chapter%2011%29%20in%20C (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### core 2 O-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000046` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00033MO`

![core 2 O-glycan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00033MO)

- **Image URL:** [G00033MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00033MO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00033MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:84263c50-3256-5701-b917-3856baedd01f`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- core 2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- core 2 O-GalNAc glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- The trisaccharide β-D-Gal-(1→3)-[β-D-GlcNAc-(1→6)]-D-GalNAc with α configuration at the anomeric centre of the N-acetylglucosamine residue at the reducing end.[CHEBI:62158] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Core 2 O-glycan is a mucin-type O-GalNAc glycan structure formed by the addition of a GlcNAcβ1-6 branch to the GalNAc residue of core 1, resulting in the structure Galβ1-3(GlcNAcβ1-6)GalNAcα1-O-Ser/Thr. This structure is a major branch of O-GalNAc glycans, is more cell type–specific than core 1, and serves as a scaffold for further extension and modification, including the formation of selectin ligands. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(b1-3)[GlcNAc(b1-6)]GalNAc(a1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch51/#:~:text=For%20O-linked%20glycan%20release%20from,of%20quantitation%20and%20detection (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=The%20same%20approach%20has%20identified,differentiation%20in%20human%20models (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- core 4 O-GalNAc glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A branched amino trisaccharide comprising an N -acetyl-α-D-galactosamine residue at the reducing end, to which are (1→3)- and (1→6)-linked two N -acetyl-α-D-glucosamine residues.[CHEBI:71341] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Core 4 O-GalNAc glycan is a mucin-type O-glycan structure where the core 3 structure (GlcNAcβ1-3GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr) is further branched by the addition of a GlcNAc residue via a β1-6 linkage, resulting in the structure GlcNAcβ1-6(GlcNAcβ1-3)GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GlcNAc(b1-3)[GlcNAc(b1-6)]GalNAc(a1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=There%20are%20two%20different%20types%20of,the%20text.%20Green%20lines%20are%20protein (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=There%20are%20two%20different%20types%20of,the%20text.%20Green%20lines%20are%20protein (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- The results confirm the predicted existence of a beta1,6GlcNAc-transferase that functions in both core 2 and core 4 O-glycan branch formation.[PMID: 9988682] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 2 references
- [PMID:10747980](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10747980/)
- [PMID:9988682](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/9988682/)

### core fucose

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000049` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G78059CC`

![core fucose](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G78059CC)

- **Image URL:** [G78059CC](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G78059CC)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G78059CC)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:50d1be21-660a-5e10-a72d-f832009f71db`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- 6-linked Fuc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Core Fuc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Fuc a1-6 to chitobiose core (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Fuc a1-6 to internal GlcNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Fuca6 to chitobiose core (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Fuca6 to internal GlcNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- a1-6 Fuc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- a6-linked Fuc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Modification of the N-glycan core structure with fucose moiety attached at the innermost GlcNAc by FUT8 enzyme. Core Fuc is found on paucimannose, hybrid and complex type N-glycans. [https://doi.org/10.1016/bs.acr.2014.11.006]. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- A fucose residue attached in α1-6 linkage to the innermost GlcNAc of N-glycans, serving as a key structural feature that influences glycan function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- Required for signaling through TCR (Source:  29434598)
- VGF, PDGFr, and EGFr and TGFbeta receptor dysregulation in Fut8 KO mice (Source: 17132494)
- IgG-Fcg receptor interactions destabilized by core Fuc (Source: 29062024)

**Disease Associations:**
- Emphysema (Source: unknown)
- systemic lupus erythematosuslung cancer (Source: unknown)
- autoimmunity (Source: unknown)
- tumor metastasis (Source: unknown)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=This%20approach%20is%20especially,to%20nonglycosylated%20proteins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- The approach was validated for selective capture and analysis of core fucosylated N-glycans present in complex glycan mixtures derived from mammalian serum IgG.[PMID:27678371] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### core M1 O-mannose glycan

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G85856KC`

![core M1 O-mannose glycan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G85856KC)

- **Image URL:** [G85856KC](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G85856KC)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G85856KC)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d8e7f221-faf6-5ef2-8636-480671b984df`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- M1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- core M1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Core M1 O-mannose glycan is one of the three main core structures (M1–M3) of O-mannose glycans, characterized by the addition of a single N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) in β1-2 linkage to a protein-linked mannose. This core structure is generated by the action of POMGNT1 and can be further extended by various glycosyltransferases, representing a specific and conserved oligosaccharide core in O-mannosylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GlcNAc(b1-2)Man(a1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Biosynthetic%20pathway%20of%20O-mannose,and%20sialyltransferases (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Core%20M1%20glycans%20can%20be%20further,that%20adds%20%CE%B21-3GalNAc%20to%20the%20GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=O-Man%20glycan%20biosynthetic%20pathway.,DPM2%2C%20and%20DPM3%20for%20Dol-P-Man%5D%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Core M2 O-mannose glycan is a type of O-mannose glycan core structure characterized by the addition of two N-acetylglucosamines (GlcNAc) to a protein-linked mannose via β1-2 and β1-6 linkages. **Description:**
- The core M3 O-mannose glycan is one of the three main core structures (M1–M3) of O-mannose glycans, representing a specific conserved oligosaccharide core found on mammalian α-dystroglycan. It serves as a priming substrate for the addition of repeating disaccharide units (Xyl-α3-GlcA-β3-)n, a process catalyzed by the LARGE1 or LARGE2 glycosyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GlcNAc(b1-4)Man(a1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=This%20disaccharide%20is%20a%20substrate,%28or%20LARGE2%29%20glycosyltransferase (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### N-acetyllactosamine (type 2)

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000129` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00055MO`

![N-acetyllactosamine (type 2)](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00055MO)

- **Image URL:** [G00055MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00055MO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00055MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:bcbf9c2c-276b-435e-a92d-848168f7bbf6`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- LacNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Type 2 LN (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- N-acetyllactosamine is a beta-D-galactopyranosyl-(1→4)-N-acetyl-D-glucosamine having beta-configuration at the reducing end anomeric centre. It is also known as galb1-4glcnacb or lacnac, belongs to the class of organic compounds known as acylaminosugars. These are organic compounds containing a sugar linked to a chain through N-acyl group. N-Acetyllactosamine is an extremely weak basic (essentially neutral) compound (based on its pKa). N-Acetyllactosamine exists in all living organisms, ranging from bacteria to humans.[CHEBI:16153,HMDB0001542] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- Utilising a fast and sensitive screening method based on imidazolium-tagged probes, we report unprecedented reversible activity of bacterial beta1,4-galactosyltransferases to catalyse the transgalactosylation from lactose to N-acetylglucosamine to form N-acetyllactosamine in the presence of UDP.[PMID:31165848] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

## Gangliosides

### GA1

**Term UUID:** `GSD:a7868da4-a6c2-4825-97b9-c86700b1c213`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- ganglioside GA1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Related Synonyms:**
- asialo-GM1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Ganglio-series asialylated (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Asialo-GM1, also known as GA1, is a neutral ganglioside of the ganglio-series with the structure Galβ1-3GalNAcβ1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer. It is formed by extending lactosylceramide with GalNAc (β1-4) followed by Gal (β1-3). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### GD1a

- ganglioside GD1a (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A branched amino hexasaccharide consisting of the linear sequence α-Neu5Ac-(2→3)-β-D-Gal-(1→3)-β-D-GalNAc-(1→4)-β-D-Gal-(1→4)-β-D-Glc having a Neu5Ac residue attached to a galactose via an α-(2→3) linkage. The oligosaccharide of ganglioside GD1a.[CHEBI:59209] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- GD1a is a ganglioside, specifically a glycosphingolipid containing two sialic acid residues. It is commonly found in brain tissue and is a member of the GD1 ganglioside family. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**

### GD1b

**Definition:**
- A branched amino hexasaccharide comprising a β-D-galactosyl-(1→4)-β-D-glucose disaccharide, the galactosyl residue of which carries β-D-galactosyl-(1→3)-N-acetyl-β-D-galactosaminyl and N-acetyl-α-neuraminyl-(2→8)-N-acetyl-α-neuraminyl disaccharide units through (2→4) and (2→3) linkages respectively. It is the carbohydrate portion of ganglioside GD1b.[CHEBI:59222] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- GD1b is a ganglioside, specifically a glycosphingolipid containing two sialic acid residues. It is predominantly found in neural tissues and is a member of the GD1 ganglioside family. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- Gangliosides have been known to play a role in the regulation of apoptosis in cancer cells. This study has employed disialyl-ganglioside GD1b to apoptosis in human breast cancer MCF-7 cells using exogenous treatment of the cells with GD1b and endogenous expression of GD1b in MCF-7 cells.[PMID: 27144558] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### GM1

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000091` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G48558GR`

![GM1](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G48558GR)

- **Image URL:** [G48558GR](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G48558GR)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G48558GR)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:3aa27544-ba80-5023-b104-e0747c328c0c`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GM1 ganglioside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- GM1-ganglioside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- ganglioside GM1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Related Synonyms:**
- GM1a (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A branched amino pentasaccharide consisting of the linear sequence β-D-Gal-(1→3)-β-D-GalNAc-(1→4)-β-D-Gal-(1→4)-β-D-Glc having a Neu5Ac residue attached to the inner galactose via an α-(2→3) linkage. The oligosaccharide of ganglioside GM1a.[CHEBI:59208] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- GM1 is a ganglioside, a type of glycosphingolipid containing one sialic acid residue. It consists of a ceramide lipid linked to a pentasaccharide glycan headgroup, which is recognized by proteins such as cholera toxin. GM1 is formed by the addition of galactose to GM2 and plays important roles as a receptor for cholera toxin and in cell signaling. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-3)[Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(b1-4)]Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- The branched pentasaccharide chain of ganglioside GM1 is a prominent cell surface ligand, for example, for cholera toxin or tumor growth-regulatory homodimeric galectins. (Source: 16267866)

**Disease Associations:**

### GM2

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000092` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

![GM2](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G79389NT)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:f05f9823-137e-586e-9ce6-90956a04b6bc`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- ganglioside GM2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A branched amino tetrasaccharide consisting of the linear sequence β-D-GalNAc-(1→4)-β-D-Gal-(1→4)-β-D-Glc having a Neu5Ac residue attached to the galactose via an α-(2→3) linkage. Corresponds to the carbohydrate portion of ganglioside GM2.[CHEBI:59220] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- GM2 is a ganglioside, a type of glycosphingolipid containing one sialic acid residue, and is found in the brain. It serves as a biosynthetic precursor to GM1 and is characterized by a sialylated glycan structure. GM2 accumulates in certain lysosomal storage diseases such as Sandhoff disease, and its degradation requires the GM2-activator protein. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### GM3

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000093` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G91237TK`

![GM3](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G91237TK)

- **Image URL:** [G91237TK](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G91237TK)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G91237TK)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:40982fbc-1026-5333-acdb-aafdf94892be`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- ganglioside GM3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Ganglioside GM3 (d18:1/16:0) is a glycosphingolipid (ceramide and oligosaccharide)or oligoglycosylceramide with one or more sialic acids (i.e. n-acetylneuraminic acid) linked on the sugar chain. It is a component the cell plasma membrane which modulates cell signal transduction events. Gangliosides have been found to be highly important in immunology. Ganglioside GM3 carries a net-negative charge at pH 7.0 and is acidic. Gangliosides can amount to 6% of the weight of lipids from brain, but they are found at low levels in all animal tissuesGangliosides are glycosphingolipids. There are four types of glycosphingolipids, the cerebrosides, sulfatides, globosides and gangliosides. Gangliosides are very similar to globosides except that they also contain N-acetyl neuraminic acid (NANA) in varying amounts. The specific names for the gangliosides provide information about their structure. The letter G refers to ganglioside, and the subscripts M, D, T and Q indicate that the molecule contains mono-, di-, tri and quatra-sialic acid. The numbered subscripts 1, 2 and 3 refer to the carbohydrate sequence that is attached to the ceramide. In particular, 1 stands for GalGalNAcGalGlc-ceramide, 2 stands for GalNAcGalGlc-ceramide and 3 stands for GalGlc-ceramide. Deficiencies in lysosomal enzymes that degrade the carbohydrate portions of various gangliosides are responsible for a number of lysosomal storage diseases such as Tay-Sachs disease, Sandhoff disease, and GM1 gangliosidosis. The carbohydrate portion of the ganglioside GM1 is the site of attachment of cholera toxin, the protein secreted by Vibrio cholerae.[HMDB0004844] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- GM3, also known as Ganglioside GM3, is the simplest member of the ganglioside series. It consists of a ceramide linked to a sialylated lactose, containing a single sialic acid residue. GM3 serves as a precursor for more complex gangliosides and is involved in modulating insulin receptor responsiveness. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**

- An amino oligosaccharide that is a branched octasaccharide derivative consisting of four sialyl residues, two galactose residues, one N-acetylglucosamine and a glucose residue at the reducing end.[CHEBI:59212] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- GQ1b is a ganglioside, a type of glycosphingolipid that contains sialic acid. It is found in cell membranes and is recognized as an antigen in certain autoimmune responses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### GT1b

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000101` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G40183QN`

![GT1b](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G40183QN)

- **Image URL:** [G40183QN](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G40183QN)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G40183QN)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:926d7349-2069-5615-b469-3b6c845ce149`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- ganglioside GT1b (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A branched amino heptasaccharide comprising a β-D-galactosyl-(1→4)-β-D-glucose disaccharide, the galactosyl residue of which carries an N-acetyl-α-neuraminyl-(2→3)-β-D-galactosyl-(1→3)-N-acetyl-β-D-galactosaminyl trisaccharide unit and an N-acetyl-α-neuraminyl-(2→8)-N-acetyl-α-neuraminyl disaccharide unit through (2→4) and (2→3) linkages respectively. It corresponds to the carbohydrate portion of ganglioside GT1b.[CHEBI:59225] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- GT1b is a ganglioside, which is a type of glycosphingolipid containing sialic acid. It functions as a docking site for specific proteins and toxins on neuronal cells. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-8)Neu5Ac(a2-3)[Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(b1-4)]Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Disease Associations:**
- Toxoplasmosis (Source: 26)
- Lyme disease (Source: 26)
- Cholera (Source: 26)
- Botulism (Source: 26)
- Tetanus (Source: 26)
- Influenza (Source: 26)

### glucosylceramide

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000090` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G71142DF`

![glucosylceramide](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G71142DF)

- **Image URL:** [G71142DF](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G71142DF)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G71142DF)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:3d06d77f-0323-508b-8678-7e86c86b85c4`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- CMH (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Ceramide monohexoside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Glc-Cer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- GlcCer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Glucocerebroside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- glucoceramide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Cerebrosides which contain as their polar head group a glucose moiety bound in glycosidic linkage to the hydroxyl group of ceramides. Their accumulation in tissue, due to a defect in beta-glucosidase, is the cause of Gaucher's disease.[MeSH:D005963] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Glucosylceramide (Glc-Cer) is a glycosphingolipid composed of a ceramide backbone with a single glucose residue attached through a β-glycosidic linkage. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- The structural characterization of mixed membranes made of cationic lipids and glucosylceramides may be important for developing novel immunotherapeutic tools such as vaccine adjuvants. (Source: 30611744)
- Glucosylceramide (GlcCer), the initial GSL synthesized from ceramide by GCS (GlcCer synthase), is required for embryonic survival, but its role in the lung is unknown. (Source: 31265321)

**Disease Associations:**
- Gaucher's Disease (Source: unknown)
- Pharyngitis (Source: 3)
- Tracheobronchitis (Source: 3)
- Pleuropneumonia (Source: 3)
- Porcine fibrinohemorrhagic necrotizing pleuropneumonia (Source: 3)
- Diarrhea (Source: 3)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch23/#:~:text=fumigatus%29%20or%20additional%20mannose,biosynthesis%20%28Chapter%2012%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=They%20typically%20result%20from,deterioration%20of%20brain%20function (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch20/#:~:text=Galactosylceramide%20and%20its,%28Chapter%2026%29%20and%20in%20mollusks (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch18/#:~:text=This%20modification%20is%20important%20for,the%20plasma%20membrane%20%28Figure%2018.1N%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Glucosylceramide (GlcCer), the initial GSL synthesized from ceramide by GCS (GlcCer synthase), is required for embryonic survival, but its role in the lung is unknown.[PMID:31265321] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### glycosylceramide

**Term UUID:** `GSD:4f42f90c-4af2-5926-95b6-d0bfead73c72`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Glycosylceramides are glycolipids consisting of a ceramide backbone linked to a short glycan chain, such as glucosylceramide (Glc-Cer) and galactosylceramide (Gal-Cer). These molecules are commonly found in fungi and play important roles in membrane structure and function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch23/#:~:text=fumigatus%29%20or%20additional%20mannose,biosynthesis%20%28Chapter%2012%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### glycosylinositolphosphorylceramide

**Term UUID:** `GSD:eb91bceb-53d2-568b-8e05-ea0447fc3a5a`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GIPCs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- glycosylinositolphosphorylceramides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Glycosylinositolphosphorylceramides (GIPCs) are plant- and fungi-specific glycolipids composed of a ceramide backbone linked to a glycosylated inositol phosphate headgroup. They are major components of the plant plasma membrane and are absent in animals. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=The%20presence%20and%20abundance%20of%20MGDG,more%20than%20200%20species%20described (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=GIPCs%20are%20estimated%20to%20comprise%20up,membranes%20remain%20to%20be%20confirmed (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=The%20presence%20and%20abundance%20of%20MGDG,more%20than%20200%20species%20described (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### lactosylceramide

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000116` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G84224TW`

![lactosylceramide](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G84224TW)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Glycosphingolipids which contain as their polar head group a lactose moiety bound in glycosidic linkage to the hydroxyl group of ceramide. Their accumulation in tissue, due to a defect in lactosylceramide beta-galactosidase, is the cause of lactosylceramidosis.[MeSH:D007790] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Lactosylceramide (LacCer) is a glycosphingolipid core structure composed of a galactose residue β1-4 linked to glucose, which is further attached to a ceramide moiety. It serves as the most common core structure of vertebrate glycosphingolipids. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- mammalian cell receptors for Neisseria gonorrhoeae recognized by Helicobacter pylori (Source: EP0078)

**Disease Associations:**
- chronic gastritis (Source: 3)
- duodenal ulcers (Source: EP0078)
- gastric adenocarcinoma (Source: 3)
- gastric ulcers (Source: EP0078)
- Pleuropneumonia (Source: 3)

### lactotriaosylceramide

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000117` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G06239NA`

![lactotriaosylceramide](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G06239NA)

- **Image URL:** [G06239NA](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G06239NA)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G06239NA)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:49675e4a-8a13-483b-a42b-96d1d2fa7139`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Lc(3)Cer (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Lc3 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Lc_3 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- A linear amino trisaccharide consisting of β-D-glucose at the reducing end having an N-acetyl-β-D-glucosaminyl-(1→3)-β-D-galactosyl group attached at the 4-position.[CHEBI:61758] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GlcNAc(b1-3)Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- acute myeloid leukemia-associated glycosphingolipids lactotriaosylceramide, GM3 and neolactotetraosylceramide are possibly involved in initiation and differentiation of acute myeloid leukemia. (Source: 22411838)

**Disease Associations:**
- Pharyngitis (Source: 20)
- Tracheobronchitis (Source: 20)
- Gonorrhea (Source: 20)
- Actinomycosis (Source: 20)
- Diarrhea (Source: 20)

**Evidence:**
- The results showed that AML patients had higher expression of the GSL lactotriaosylceramide (Lc3), GM3 and neolactotetraosylceramide (nLc4) in their bone marrow than did the healthy donors (P &lt; 0.05), especially the M1 subtype of AML.[PMID:22411838] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### Manα1-2Manα1-2Manα1-6Manα1-4GlcN-inositolphosphoceramide

**Classification:**
- IUPAC String (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- This is a glycosylphosphoceramide structure identified in Aspergillus fumigatus, featuring a galactomannan chain with a defined mannosylated core (Manα1-2Manα1-2Manα1-6Manα1-4GlcN) that is directly linked to inositolphosphoceramide, notably lacking a bridging phosphoethanolamine. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### polyglycosylceramide

**Term UUID:** `GSD:32f8e4bf-4bce-5880-822c-2bbe9e194237`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Polyglycosylceramides are a class of glycolipids, also known as macroglycolipids, characterized by long glycan chains—often containing poly-N-acetyllactosamine—attached to a ceramide lipid anchor. These molecules can carry blood group antigens such as A, B, and H. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

### 6-sulfo-sialyl Lewis x

- 6-sulfo SLex (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- 6-sulfo-sialyl Lewis X (6-sulfo-SLex) is a sialyl Lewis X (LeX) glycan motif that is further modified by sulfation at the 6-position of the N-acetylglucosamine. As a high-affinity L-selectin ligand (often on mucin-type glycoconjugates), it promotes leukocyte rolling and homing at high endothelial venules and can influence cell–cell interactions in inflammation and cancer.) (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc6S(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- lymphocyte homing (Source: EP0015)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Sialyl-6-sulfo-Lewis%20x%20and,portion%20of%20glycosphingolipids (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Similar%20interactions%20involving,and%20facilitate%20tumor%20progression (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- The cognate ligands for L-selectin possess the unusual sulfated tetrasaccharide epitope 6-sulfo sialyl Lewis x (Siaalpha2--&gt;3Galbeta1--&gt;4[Fucalpha1--&gt;3][SO(3)--&gt;6]GlcNAc).[PMID:11331001] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

- **Image URL:** [G00672PE](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00672PE)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:6e033c8a-f202-509b-8b71-6eef358777b2`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- 6'-Sulfo-sialyl Lewis x (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- 6'-sulfated sialyl Lewis x- (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- 6'-sulfo-sLe(x) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- 6′-sulfo-SLex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Sialyl-6′-sulfo-Lewis x (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Su-SaiLex (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Su-Slex (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- sialyl-6′-sulfo-Lex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A branched amino tetrasaccharide comprised of a trisaccharide chain of N-acetyl-α-neuraminic acid, 6-O-sulfo-β-D-glactose and N-acetyl-β-D-glucosamine residues linked sequentially (2→3) and (1→4), to the GlcNAC residue of which is also linked (1→3) an α-L-fucose residue.[CHEBI:71558] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- 6′-sulfo-sialyl Lewis X (6′-sulfo-SLex) is a sialyl Lewis X (SLex) glycan motif that is further modified by sulfation at the 6′ position of the galactose residue. It serves as a recognition ligand for selectins and Siglecs, guiding cell to cell interactions in the immune system and shaping inflammatory responses at mucosal surfaces. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal6S(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- lymphocyte homing (Source: EP0014)

**Disease Associations:**
- Influenza (Source: 128)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch35/#:~:text=It%20has%20also%20been%20shown%20that,such%20as%20interleukin-5%20%28IL-5%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch35/#:~:text=Apoptosis%20depends%20on%20generation%20of,using%20different%20signaling%20pathways (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Studies using sulfotransferase-deficient mice showed that 6-sulfo sialyl Lewis X (6-sulfo sLe(x)), a major ligand for L-selectin that is expressed on the high endothelial venules (HEVs), plays critical roles in lymphocyte homing to the peripheral lymph nodes.[PMID:2288521] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### disialyl Lewis a

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000063` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G93400AK`

![disialyl Lewis a](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G93400AK)

- **Image URL:** [G93400AK](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G93400AK)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G93400AK)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:2c92929a-4f34-5166-a815-b20e3decb896`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- disialyl Lea (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- disialyl-Lewis a (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Disialyl Lewis is a sialyl Lewis a with an additional sialic acid attached at the C-6 position of penultimate GlcNAc. [PMID:15231659] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- A glycan motif composed of the Lewis a antigen with two sialic acid residues attached. It is present on colonic epithelial cells and its expression is altered in colon cancer. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-3)[Neu5Ac(a2-6)][Fuc(a1-4)]GlcNAc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- The 2-3,2-6 disialyl Lea determinant on epithelial cells may play a role in protecting normal epithelial cells. (Source: EP0040)

**Disease Associations:**
- Mucin antigen carrying 2-3 sialylated Le a antigen or 2-3, 2-6 disialylated Lc4 antigen in cancer patients had the lowest carbohydrate/protein ratio. Thus, the carbohydrate/protein ratio in the type 1 chain mucin antigens in sera of normal subjects is higher than that in sera of cancer patients. (Source: EP0040)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Sialyl-6-sulfo-Lewis%20x%20and,portion%20of%20glycosphingolipids (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Biosynthesis of disialyl Lewis a (Lea) was analyzed using previously cloned ST6GalNAc V and ST6GalNAc VI, which were responsible for the synthesis of alpha-series gangliosides.[PMID:12668675] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### Lewis a

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000119` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00047MO`, `G39023AU`

![Lewis a](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00047MO)

- **Image URL:** [G00047MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00047MO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00047MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:c5a61542-ca29-5ce2-bf63-a4f7caf9bf9c`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Le(a) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Le^a (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Lea (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Lea-3 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A lewis blood group antigen with structure beta-D-Galp-(1-&gt;3)-[alpha-L-Fucp-(1-&gt;4)]-D-GlcpNAc consisting of N-acetylglucosamine having a fucosyl residue attached at the 4-position via an alpha-linkage and a galactosyl residue attached at the 3-position via a beta-linkage. It has a role as an epitope and an antigen. It is an amino trisaccharide and a glucosamine oligosaccharide.[CHEBI:62265] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Lewis a is a fucosylated type 1 chain glycan epitope with the structure Galβ1-3(Fucα1-4)GlcNAc, commonly referred to as the Lewis a antigen. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(b1-3)[Fuc(a1-4)]GlcNAc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Disease Associations:**
- Gastroenteritis (Source: 155)

**Evidence:**

- Regardless of the vaccine administered, significantly fewer of the children with Lewis A phenotype (0/14) seroconverted after receiving the first vaccine dose compared to 26% (45/175) of those with the Lewis B phenotype and 32% (15/47) of the Lewis negative individuals (P &lt; 0.01). [PMID:29367698] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### Lewis antigens

**Term UUID:** `GSD:cabb56b2-34c2-5250-be10-65af64594104`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Lewis antigens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Lewis blood group antigens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Lewis type glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Lewis-type structures (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Lewis antigens are a family of fucosylated glycan epitopes, including Lewis a, b, x, and y, that function as blood group antigens and mediate cell-cell interactions. These structures are characterized by the addition of L-fucosyl residues to a glycan backbone, resulting in branched motifs commonly found on glycoproteins and glycolipids. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=This%20is%20because%20H.%20pylori%20binds,with%20an%20ABO-incompatible%20type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=%28more...%29%20Expression%20of%20Lea%20and,a%20hemolytic%20transfusion%20reaction (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=The%20cattle%20parasite%20D.%20viviparous,Examples%20of%20nematode%20glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Because%20of%20structural,function%20in%20the%20pathogenesis%20of%20H (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch46/#:~:text=Numerous%20gastrointestinal,explaining%20this%20association (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- *...and 1 more evidence entries*

- **Image URL:** [G00051MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00051MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:89ba627c-cee9-5954-982f-3410e75fb006`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Galbeta1-4(Fucalpha1-3)GlcNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Le(X) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Le^x (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Lewis x trisaccharide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Lex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- SSEA-1 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A Lewis blood group antigen with structure α-L-Fucp-(1→3)-[β-D-Galp-(1→4)]-D-GlcpNAc where the glucosamine at the reducing end has β-configuration at its anomeric centre. Commonly known as Lewis x trisaccharide or Lex.[CHEBI:59294] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Lewis x (Lex) is a fucosylated type 2 chain glycan epitope with the structure Galβ1-4(Fucα1-3)GlcNAc. It is found on N-glycans, O-glycans, and glycolipids in animals and some plants. Glycans containing the Lewis x motif are characterized by the addition of this fucosylated N-acetyllactosamine structure to the glycan chain. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- Involved in carbohydrate-carbohydrate interaction[Glycoepitope:EP0011]. (Source: unknown)
- Also implicated in apoptosis. (Source: EP0011)
- influence antigenic presentation and interfere with pathogen transfer in breastfed infants. (Source: EP0011)

**Disease Associations:**
- acute leukemia (Source: EP0011)
- lymphoma (Source: EP0011)

### Lewis y

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000122` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00052MO`

![Lewis y](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00052MO)

- **Image URL:** [G00052MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00052MO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00052MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:c05f267e-fa66-4b1a-bd6c-4a250acb5376`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Le(y) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- LeY (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Le^y (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Ley (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- A Lewis blood group antigen which is a branched amino tetrasaccharide with structure alpha-L-Fucp-(1-&gt;2)-beta-D-Galp-(1-&gt;4)-[alpha-L-Fucp-(1-&gt;3)]-beta-D-GlcpNAc, consisting of consisting of N-acetyl-beta-D-glucosamine at the reducing end having an alpha-L-fucosyl residue attached at the 3-position and an alpha-L-fucosyl-(1→2)-beta-D-galactosyl moiety attached at the 4-position. A cancer-associated tetrasaccharide antigen which constitutes the core structure recognised by therapeutic antibody BR96. Conformational restriction around the GlcNAc residue is a key feature of its structure. It has a role as an antigen and an epitope. [CHEBI:59045]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Fuc(a1-2)Gal(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- The expression of the fucosylated histo-blood group antigen CD174 in CD34+ hematopoietic progenitor cells and down-regulation of FUT1 in mature lymphocytes may be important factors influencing the homing process of hematopoietic stem cells to the bone marrow. (Source: EP0018)

**Disease Associations:**
- AIDS/acquired immune deficiency syndrome (Source: EP0018)
- cholangiocarcinoma (Source: EP0018)
- hepatocellular carcinoma (Source: EP0018)

**Evidence:**
- In conclusion, Lewis y antigen and CD147 were significantly upregulated in ovarian tumors, and the altered expression of Lewis y may cause changes in CD147.[PMID:30816446] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### sialyl Lewis a

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000150` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00053MO`

![sialyl Lewis a](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00053MO)

- **Image URL:** [G00053MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00053MO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00053MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:89e36335-2fea-5ef3-a3a8-559985883dad`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- CA19-9 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- CA19-9 antigen (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- SLe^a (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- SLea (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Slea (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- sLeA (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- sLea (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- sialyl-Lea (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Related Synonyms:**
- CA19-9 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A sialyated version of Lewis A with a branched structure of amino tetrasaccharide consisting of a sialyl residue, linked (2→3) to a galactosyl residue that in turn is linked (1→3) to a glucosaminyl residue at the reducing end, which is also carrying a fucosyl residue at the 4-position.[CHEBI:62681] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Sialyl Lewis a (sLea) is a tetrasaccharide glycan motif with the structure Neu5Acα2-3Galβ1-3(Fucα1-4)GlcNAc. It serves as a binding determinant for E-selectin and is recognized as a biomarker in cancer diagnostics, notably detected by the CA19-9 assay. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-3)[Fuc(a1-4)]GlcNAc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- The carbohydrate determinants,Sialyl Lea and Sialyl Lex,which are frequently expressed on human cancer cells,serve as ligands for a cell adhesion molecule of the selectin family,E-selectin,which is expressed on vascular endothelial cells. These carbohydrate determinants are involved in the adhesion of cancer cells to vascular endothelium and thus contribute to hematogenous metastasis of cancer. (Source: EP0008)

**Disease Associations:**
- pancreatic adenocarcinoma (Source: EP0008)
- hepatocellular cancer (Source: EP0008)
- cholangiocellular cancer (Source: EP0008)
- gastric cancer (Source: unknown)
- [GlycoEpitope:EP0008]colorectal cancer (Source: EP0008)
- ovarian cancer (Source: EP0008)
- lung cancer (Source: EP0008)
- breast cancer (Source: EP0008)
- uterine cancer (Source: EP0008)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=HIF-1%CE%B1%20induces%20transcription%20of,expressed%20by%20cancer%20stem%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=EMT%20is%20a%20critical%20event%20in%20tumor,are%20directed%20by%20O-GlcNAcylation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=EMT%20is%20typified%20by%20alterations%20in,used%20in%20monitoring%20ovarian%20cancer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- By comparing the serum O-glycan profiles from healthy controls with those of cancer patients, we identified a marker candidate, core 1 sialyl Lewis A (NeuAcα2-3Galβ1-3(Fucα1-4)GlcNAcβ1-3Gal) (abbreviated C1SLA), whose concentration appeared to be weakly correlated with CA19-9 values.[PMID:26641888] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### sialyl Lewis x

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000151` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00054MO`

![sialyl Lewis x](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00054MO)

- **Image URL:** [G00054MO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00054MO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00054MO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:32e928fb-1550-5e0a-945f-2218ac79b83c`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- SLe(x) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- SLe^x (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- SLex (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Slex (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- sLeX (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- sLex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- sialyl-Lex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A sialylated version of Lewis X antigen expressed on cell surfaces with structure alpha-Neup5Ac-(2-&gt;3)-beta-D-Galp-(1-&gt;4)-[alpha-L-Fucp-(1-&gt;3)]-beta-D-GlcpNAcis α-Neup5Ac-(2→3)-β-D-Galp-(1→4)-[α-L-Fucp-(1→3)]-D-GlcpNAc in which the anomeric configuration of the reducing-end N-acetyl-beta-D-glucosamine residue is beta. It is a ligand for selectins. [MESH:2030970,CHEI:71622] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Sialyl Lewis x (sLex) is a tetrasaccharide glycan motif with the structure Neu5Acα2-3Galβ1-4(Fucα1-3)GlcNAc. It is a well-known selectin ligand involved in cell adhesion, particularly mediating leukocyte rolling, trafficking, and extravasation during inflammation and immune responses. sLex can be found as a terminal cap on both N- and O-glycans, including core 2 O-glycans, and is used as a biomarker in various biological and pathological processes such as hematopoiesis and leukemogenesis. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- Sialyl Lex was shown to be a specific ligand on human leukocytes for vascular E- and P-selectins,and was shown to mediate leukocyte recruitment. (Source: EP0012)
- The determinant is also frequently expressed on human cancer cells,and is involved in the adhesion of cancer cells to vascular endothelium and thus contribute to hematogenous metastasis of cancer. (Source: EP0012)

**Disease Associations:**
- colon cancer (Source: EP0012)
- colorectal cancer (Source: EP0012)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=HIF-1%CE%B1%20induces%20transcription%20of,expressed%20by%20cancer%20stem%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=EMT%20is%20a%20critical%20event%20in%20tumor,are%20directed%20by%20O-GlcNAcylation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch46/#:~:text=Some%20of%20these%20changes%20have%20been,consisting%20predominantly%20of%20mucins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=In%20leukocytes%2C%20PSGL-1%20is%20enriched,death%20protein%201%20%28PD-1%29%20expression (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch58/#:~:text=Dextran%20was%20also%20employed%20to,of%20apoptosis%20of%20metastatic%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### high-mannose N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000108` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G55220VL`

![high-mannose N-glycan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G55220VL)

- **Image URL:** [G55220VL](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G55220VL)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G55220VL)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:bd55e967-56fc-57a8-a669-0f45c0eb9a21`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- high mannose glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- high-mannose-type N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- oligomannose N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- oligomannose, high-mannose, oligomannose-type (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- oligomannose-type N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- oligomannosidic glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- oligomannosyl N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- An N-linked oligosaccharide that possesses between five and nine mannose residues attached to the chitobiose [(GlcNAc)2] core. [CHEBI:5717] None of the Man residues are substituted with any monosaccharide other than Man . (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- High-mannose N-glycans, also known as oligomannose-type glycans, are a class of N-linked glycans characterized by a core pentasaccharide (Man3GlcNAc2) that is extended exclusively with additional mannose residues, typically resulting in structures with five to nine mannose units. These glycans are biosynthesized in the endoplasmic reticulum from dolichol-linked precursors and are found on glycoproteins, where they play important roles in protein folding, quality control, trafficking, and recognition. High-mannose N-glycans can serve as ligands for lectins, are substrates for enzymes such as endo H, and may be further modified, for example by phosphorylation during lysosomal targeting. They lack complex antennae and are notable for their strong binding to ConA-agarose. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- Involved in promotion of protein folding in ER. [PMID:12417032]. (Source: unknown)
- High Man glycans are enriched at cell surface during apoptosis. (Source:  17516205)

**Disease Associations:**
- Relative abundance changes in multiple CDGs [PMID: 30770376] (Source:  28484880)

**Evidence:**

### paucimannose N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000144` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:146aba34-600b-5250-8eaa-78b19e76b286`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Man2-3GlcNAc2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- paucimannose (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- paucimannose glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- paucimannose-type glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- paucimannosidic N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- paucimannosidic glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Any N-linked glycan derivative with composition Man1-4Fuc0-1 GlcNAc2, in which the chitobiose core (GlcNAc2) is intact. Found in relatively large amounts in invertebrates and plants, they are formed by removal of the non-reducing terminal GlcNAc (\added by GlcNAcT1) from GlcNAcMan3-5GlcNAc2 by a Golgi hexosaminidase and subsequent removal of Man residues by Golgi mannosidases. Pauscimannose glycans have also been detected in vertebrates.[CHEBI:144386] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Paucimannose N-glycans are a class of truncated N-linked glycans characterized by a chitobiose core (GlcNAc2) and a reduced number of mannose residues, typically two or three, but sometimes up to five (e.g., Man2–5GlcNAc2). These structures often lack sialic acids and other anionic groups, and may be modified by core α1-6-fucosylation or other substitutions. Paucimannose N-glycans arise from extensive trimming of high-mannose N-glycans during glycan processing, involving the action of Golgi β-hexosaminidases and α-mannosidase, resulting in shorter glycan chains compared to complex or oligomannose N-glycans. They are commonly found in insects, plants, some invertebrates, and occasionally in mammals. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=In%20terms%20of%20Golgi%20modifications%20of,and%20the%20Co%2B%2B-dependent%20AMAN-3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=The%20baculovirus-insect%20cell,add%20terminal%20sialic%20acids (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=GlcNAcT-I%20catalyzes%20the%20first,have%20not%20been%20characterized (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=Brain%20lobes%2C%20normally%20separated%20in,activity%20and%20decreased%20life%20span (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### terminal fucose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:caf303a2-143f-51b8-887a-9fdc7e34a48c`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- terminal Fuc residue (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan structure featuring a fucose residue at the non-reducing terminus of glycan chains, as found in H and Lewis antigens. These terminal fucose residues can be cleaved by fucosidases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=They%20show%20robust%20titers%20of%20anti-H%2C,such%20as%20the%20H%20and%20Lewis%20antigens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch42/#:~:text=When%20dietary%20fucose%20is%20low%2C%20the,are%20missing%20from%20the%20diet (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### terminal galactose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:0c32fd16-6ba8-5f21-aacc-e67e0b71acc1`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- exposed galactose (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- terminal Gal residue (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A galactose residue located at the non-reducing end of a glycan, often exposed after the removal of terminal sialic acids. This structural feature can influence glycan recognition and function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch28/#:~:text=Like%20most%20glycoproteins,or%20GlcNAc%20residues%20from%20the%20blood (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch28/#:~:text=Once%20released%20from%20cells%2C,degradation%20of%20cellulose%20fibers (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=St3gal5%20is%20expressed%20well%20in,receptor%20%28Chapter%2034%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=In%20normal%20physiology%20platelets%2C,of%20SLex%20and%20SLea%20in%20cancers (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch37/#:~:text=Whereas%20these%20secondary,to%20manage%20E.%20histolytica%20infection (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- Glucuronic acid residues present at the non-reducing end of glycan chains, commonly observed in certain N-glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### terminal mannose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:9b46c1bc-c89c-51be-a6bd-cb98e416888c`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- terminal Man (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Mannose residues located at the non-reducing end of glycan chains, which are important for recognition by mannose receptors. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

### terminal α-linked fucose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:57fe1544-36ee-52f8-9802-d4ffc1867028`

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan feature characterized by the presence of an α-linked fucose residue at the nonreducing end of a glycan chain. This structural motif is recognized by certain lectins and adhesion molecules, playing important roles in molecular recognition and cell signaling. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=Interestingly%2C%20different%20regions,%E2%80%9Cself-antigens%E2%80%9D%20from%20pathogens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### terminal α-linked mannose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:a7da3922-7453-5cc0-897b-4bb463ece798`

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan feature where α-linked mannose is present at the nonreducing end of a glycan chain; this motif is recognized by certain lectins and adhesion molecules. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=Interestingly%2C%20different%20regions,%E2%80%9Cself-antigens%E2%80%9D%20from%20pathogens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### terminal α-linked-galactose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:ddda1d65-b417-51b2-8b8a-d5e9e86509b5`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- terminal α-galactose residue (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan feature characterized by the presence of α-linked galactose at the nonreducing end of a glycan chain. The occurrence or absence of terminal α-galactose can be used to distinguish glycan types among different species or cell lines. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=More%20details%20about%20glycosylation,with%20plates%20coated%20with%20the%20lectin (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### terminal α1-6 mannose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:8759d2e6-5d43-508d-bec0-a9cdf395b049`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- terminal α1–6Man (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan feature where a mannose residue is present at the nonreducing end of a glycan chain, linked via an α1-6 glycosidic bond. This terminal α1-6 mannose is important for recognition by specific endoplasmic reticulum lectins and plays a role in ER-associated degradation pathways. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch39/#:~:text=Retrotranslocation%20of%20misfolded,by%20Hrd3p%20in%20the%20Hrd1%20complex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch39/#:~:text=Mammalian%20cells%20have%2C%20in%20addition,Yos9p%20in%20Saccharomyces%20cerevisiae%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch39/#:~:text=The%20M7BC%20isomer%20exposes%20an%20%CE%B11%E2%80%936Man,protein%20called%20Htm1p%20are%20present (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan feature characterized by the presence of β-linked galactose at the nonreducing end of a glycan chain. This terminal structure can serve as a substrate for sialylation or be recognized by specific lectins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch43/#:~:text=These%20GPI-anchored%20proteins%20form%20a,these%20are%20far%20less%20well%20studied.%20T (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### tri-mannose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:e42c7aa4-8b95-5f1c-8fe3-c263093d7b5a`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- tri-Man (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan composed of three mannose residues, found as a modification on intracellular proteins in Saccharomyces cerevisiae. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### α(1-6)-linked glucose branch

**Term UUID:** `GSD:10dc47f9-89f6-59b5-aa58-22fd27d80b6e`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- periodic α1–6Glc branch (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Branches in glycogen that consist of glucose units connected by α(1–6) glycosidic bonds, appearing periodically along the main α(1–4)-linked glucose chain. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch05/#:~:text=Glycan%20salvage%20pathways%20are,by%20phosphorolysis%20of%20glycogen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### α-linked O-fucose modification

**Term UUID:** `GSD:c8753ce2-90c7-5f6d-8408-8771b0fbcdd3`

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A post-translational modification in which a fucose residue is attached through an alpha linkage to the oxygen atom of serine or threonine residues, most commonly found on epidermal growth factor (EGF) repeats. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

## O-Linked Glycans

### O-acetylated sialic acids

**Term UUID:** `GSD:929ed22f-b4a5-54aa-9c0d-8000b75e4a33`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- O-acetylated Sias (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Sialic acids that are modified by the addition of an O-acetyl group at one or more hydroxyl positions. These modifications can influence recognition by viral and immune receptors and are regulated by specific esterases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch42/#:~:text=HIV%20expresses%20surface%20glycans%20that,the%20spike%20receptor%20binding%20domain (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch46/#:~:text=The%20primary%20cause%20of%20the%20disease,dialysis%20and%20open-heart%20surgery (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=However%2C%20at%20the%20whole-body%20level%2C,are%20released%20from%20host%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=Mutations%20of%20other%20STs%20in%20mice,to%20protect%20against%20microorganisms (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### O-fucose glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000137` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G39294YF`

![O-fucose glycan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G39294YF)

- **Image URL:** [G39294YF](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G39294YF)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G39294YF)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:90a94f3e-97fc-5377-bba5-0d13d2766193`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Form of glycosylation where the protein is modified by a fucose residue and can be extended futher to form a tetrasaccharide in certain cases. alpha-linked o-fucose have a consensus motif of C2X4(s/T)C3 where C2 and C3 are conserved cysteines number 2 and 3 respectively of the EGFR like repeats. [Essentials of Glycobiology:Chapter13] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- O-fucose glycans are glycans in which fucose is O-linked to serine or threonine residues, most notably found on Notch receptors. These structures play important roles in regulating signaling pathways that are critical for cell differentiation and development. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- modification of Notch signaling. (Source: EP0005)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=The%20gene%20encoding%20the%20O-fucose-EGF,of%20loss%20of%20Notch%20signaling (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Elimination%20of%20POFUT1%20in,folding%20and%20trafficking%20of%20Notch (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=The%20fucose%20on%20NOTCH1%20EGF12,disaccharide%20forms%20are%20also%20seen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch41/#:~:text=Leukocyte%20deficiency%20of%20SLex,stem%20cell%20niche%20%28Chapter%2013%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch41/#:~:text=The%20O-fucose%20glycans%20on%20Notch,cells%20%28Chapter%2034%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### O-GalNAc core structures

**Term UUID:** `GSD:7cee9dd6-9c05-54fc-947b-afca69f08b9e`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- O-GalNAc cores (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- cores 1–4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- O-GalNAc core structures are conserved oligosaccharide cores formed by the extension of the initial O-linked GalNAc residue on serine or threonine. These structures, known as cores 1–4, serve as the foundational building blocks for complex O-GalNAc glycans, particularly in mucins, and are essential for O-glycan biosynthesis in vertebrates. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=However%2C%20core%203%20and%204%20O-GalNAc,sugars%20or%20functional%20groups (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=The%20first%20mucin%20polypeptide%20gene,structures%20%28cores%201%E2%80%934%3B%20Table%2010.1%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Also%20discussed%20are%20milk,%28LacNAc%29%20%28Figure%2014.2%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=%28more...%29%20A%20number%20of%20inhibitors,to%20another%20is%20often%20difficult (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### O-GalNAc glycans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:fa47b325-2aab-42f9-9d2d-b2499fab770c`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GalNAc-type O-glycans (CLASS) (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- O-GalNAc glycans are a class of O-linked glycans initiated by the attachment of N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) to serine or threonine residues on proteins. They are commonly found in mucins and other glycoproteins, and can be extended to form diverse structures, including blood group and Lewis antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=For%20example%2C%20deletion%20of%20the%20gene,normal%20cell-derived%20glycoproteins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Examples%20of%20this%20include%20the%20hinge,O-glycan%20engineering%20in%20yeast (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=Sialic%20acids%20may%20be%20modified%20by,model%20of%20a%20large%20secreted%20mucin (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=A%20simplified%20model%20of%20a%20large,and%20biological%20properties (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=In%20mucins%2C%20O-GalNAc%20glycans,bronchial%20and%20lung%20diseases (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### O-GalNAc-linked sialoglycans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:365d7f89-b85d-538a-b378-d8b369d75d62`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- O-GalNAc-linked sialoglycans are a type of O-GalNAc glycan that have been further modified by the addition of sialic acid residues. These structures are commonly found on mucins and other glycoproteins, where the sialic acid imparts extended and negatively charged properties to the glycan chains. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=These%20functions%20are%20not%20mutually,collapse%20without%20glycosylation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### O-glucose glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d740545e-dea4-57d3-8167-6524292d59ac`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- O-Glc glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- O-Glc glycans are glycans composed of a single glucose residue that is O-linked to serine or threonine residues of proteins. This type of O-glycosylation is found in various glycoconjugates and represents a simple form of protein glycosylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Large%20libraries%20of%20isogenic%20cells,and%20TALEN-mediated%20gene%20knockout (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### O-glycans

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000139` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d0dfe91d-737b-59d2-9fa5-746a0d0ed6ad`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- O-linked oligosaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Ser/Thr-linked glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- The carbohydrate portion of a glycoprotein that has a glycan linked through the hydroxyl oxygen of serine, threonine, hydroxylysine or hydroxyproline side-chains.[CHEBI:59521] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- O-glycans are a class of glycans covalently attached to the serine or threonine residues of proteins via O-glycosidic bonds. Common in animals, they display diverse core structures often containing GalNAc, GlcNAc, Gal, fucose, and sialic acid. O-glycans are synthesized in the Golgi apparatus and play important roles in protein function, stability, and cell-cell interactions. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- Affects several important activities like protein stability, modulation of signaling molecules and enzyme activity. (Source: 9673446)

### O-linked fucose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:0b39b32b-2f19-537b-a733-8b030a6cf9dd`

- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- O-linked fucose refers to a glycan modification in which a fucose residue is attached via an O-linkage to serine or threonine residues of proteins. This modification is particularly important in modulating developmental signaling pathways, such as those involving the Notch receptor. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

This modification is a dynamic post-translational modification found in multicellular eukaryotes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
