### O-mannose glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000140` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G59126YU`

![O-mannose glycan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G59126YU)

- **Image URL:** [G59126YU](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G59126YU)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G59126YU)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:7f2b0e5a-45b8-5366-9e6d-892f75c98d6a`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- O-Man glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- O-mannose initiated glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- O-mannose-based glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- O-mannosyl glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- O-linked glycans that are initiated by the addtion of a Man residue to Ser or Thr. Subsequent extension of the Man defines the O-Man core type, either M1, M2, or M3. The M1 type is initated by addition of a GlcNAc in β-linkage to the 2 position of the Man residue. M2 is initiated by addition of another GlcNAc to the 6 position of the M1 core. M3 is initiated by the addition of a GlcNAc in β-linkage to the 4 position of the Man residue. M1 and M2 type core glycans can be extended and branched with Gal, Fuc, Sialic Acid, and GlcA. The M3 core is uniquely extended by a multienzyme system to generate the matriglycan polysaccharide, a protein-specific modification found only on alpha-dystroglycan. [CHEBI:87761] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- O-mannose glycans are a class of O-glycans initiated by the attachment of mannose to serine or threonine residues of proteins. Found in mammals, yeast, and other organisms, these glycans can be extended into several core structures, such as core M1 and M2. They play important roles in protein function, cell signaling, and act as receptors for viruses and extracellular matrix proteins. In mammals, O-mannose glycans are especially abundant in the brain and on α-dystroglycan, with defects in their biosynthesis linked to congenital muscular dystrophies. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Disease Associations:**
- MEB/muscle-eye-brain disease (Source: EP0002)
- WWS/walker-warburg syndrome (Source: unknown)
- muscular dystrophy (Source: EP0002)
- neuronal migration disorder (Source: EP0002)
- Muscle-Eye-Brain disease (MEB) and Walker-Warburg Syndrome (WWS) have mutations in genes encoding glycosyltransferases needed for O -mannosyl oligosaccharide synthesis (Source: EP0002)
- Muscular dystrophy and neuronal migration disorder are caused by mutations in a glycosyltransferase, POMGnT1. (Source: EP0002)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=NGLY1%20defects%20do%20not%20appear%20to,results%20in%20muscular%20dystrophy (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=The%20pathway%20is%20initiated%20in%20the%20ER,O-Man%20glycans%20are%20shown (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=O-Man%20glycan%20biosynthetic%20pathway.,DPM2%2C%20and%20DPM3%20for%20Dol-P-Man%5D%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch49/#:~:text=The%20many%20CHO%20mutants%20have%20recently,these%20glycans%20to%20be%20truncated (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### O-xylose-linked glycosaminoglycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:a9cdd507-5984-5398-a41a-79a9dc01deb4`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- O-xylose-linked GAG chains (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycosaminoglycan (GAG) chain that is covalently attached to a core protein through an O-linked xylose residue. This structure includes dermatan sulfate (DS), chondroitin sulfate (CS), and heparan sulfate (HS), all of which share a common core tetrasaccharide linkage region. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

## Other Glycans

### 2-6-branch of complex N-glycans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:3b67a794-e241-56bb-b993-661b1a47f3ef`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- 2-6-branch (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- The 2-6-branch of complex N-glycans refers to the α1-6 core mannose that is substituted at both the 2 and 6 positions, bearing GlcNAcβ1-6 and GlcNAcβ1-2 antennae. This structure typically features a Galβ1-4GlcNAcβ1-2(Galβ1-4GlcNAcβ1-6)Manα1-R motif, representing a common branching pattern in complex N-glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=A%20good%20example%20of%20this%20is%20the,%28MGAT5%20and%20-5B%29%20%28Chapter%209%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- (2S,4S,5R,6R)-5-Acetamido-2-[(2R,3S,4R,5R,6S)-3-[(2S,3R,4R,5R,6R)-3-acetamido-4-[(2R,3R,4S,5R,6R)-4,5-dihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)-3-[(2S,3S,4R,5S,6S)-3,4,5-trihydroxy-6-methyloxan-2-yl]oxyoxan-2-yl]oxy-5-hydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-2-yl]oxy-5-hydroxy-2-(hydroxymethyl)-6-[(2R,3S,4R,5R)-4,5,6-trihydroxy-2-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-3-yl]oxyoxan-4-yl]oxy-4-hydroxy-6-[(1R,2R)-1,2,3-trihydroxypropyl]oxane-2-carboxylic acid.[CHEBI:151476] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-3)[Fuc(a1-2)Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(b1-4)]Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**
- These glycolipid products were identified as asialo-Gs.., GM h and fucosyl-Gq, by comparison with authentic glycolipid samples.[PMID:3654655] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 1 references
- [PMID:3654655](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/3654655/)

### 2′-fucosyllactose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:10ae2b99-1d75-5290-a777-47b7b1fcaf32`

**Classification:**
- Disaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- 2′-fucosyllactose is a human milk oligosaccharide composed of lactose with an α1-2-linked fucose attached to the galactose residue. It is known to inhibit pathogen binding by mimicking host cell surface receptors. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch40/#:~:text=The%20amount%20and%20composition%20of%20HMOs,and%20modulate%20cytokine%20production (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- To determine whether 9-O-acetyl GD3 (acGD3) has a general antiapoptotic potential, the apoptosis-sensitive Jurkat cell line and an apoptosis-sensitive variant of the cell line Molt-4 were preincubated with micromolar concentrations of acGD3 and then treated with inducers of apoptosis.[PMID:16432837] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### a-series gangliosides

**Term UUID:** `GSD:70869a6d-1921-5736-8f3a-af3e124c381a`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- a-series gangliosides are a class of glycosphingolipids characterized by the presence of a single sialic acid residue attached to the inner galactose of the lactosylceramide core. They are biosynthesized through the addition of sialic acid to GM3 and cannot be directly converted to b-series gangliosides by sialyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=Each%20branch%20is%20a%20committed,by%20a%20single%20galactosyltransferase (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- A glycan structure that is attached to a membrane through an alkylacylphosphatidylinositol anchor. This glycan may contain components such as nonacetylated glucosamine, mannose, galactofuranose, and 2-aminoethylphosphonate. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch43/#:~:text=For%20T.%20cruzi%20sialylation%20is,of%20chemotherapeutic%20agents (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### arthroseries glycosphingolipids

**Term UUID:** `GSD:44424bf0-a3e2-524d-9b57-0879a7c56c2b`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- arthro-series (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- arthroseries (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- arthroseries glycolipids (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A family of glycosphingolipids (GSLs) characteristic of invertebrates, especially arthropods, built on a mactosylceramide core (Manβ1-4Glcβ-ceramide) that may contain multiple N-acetylglucosamine residues and is often further modified with galactose, fucose, glucuronic acid, phosphoethanolamine, or phosphorylcholine. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=dentatum%20%28Figure%2025.8%29.%20Examples,of%20mammalian%20innate%20immune%20systems (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=However%2C%20arthropods%20possess%20their,core%20%28Gal%CE%B21-4Glc%CE%B2-ceramide%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=The%20functional%20significance%20of,as%20modulators%20of%20cell%20fate (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### b-series gangliosides

**Term UUID:** `GSD:e21a336f-6519-5fe7-9429-bcfa0d13f084`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- b-series (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- b-series gangliosides are a class of glycosphingolipids characterized by the presence of two sialic acid residues attached to the inner galactose. Examples include GD3 and GT1b. These structures are formed through the action of sialyltransferases during glycosphingolipid biosynthesis. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=The%20biosynthesis%20of%20GSLs%20in%20the,of%20the%20%E2%80%9Cb-series%E2%80%9D%20gangliosides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch35/#:~:text=Recombinant%20forms%20of%20MAG%20bind,which%20lack%20GT1b%20but%20express%20GD1a (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch35/#:~:text=Siglec-7%20is%20the%20major%20Siglec%20on,6-sulfo-SLex%20and%206%E2%80%B2-sulfo-SLex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=Each%20branch%20is%20a%20committed,by%20a%20single%20galactosyltransferase (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### biantennary complex-type N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000023` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:7548e42d-e075-5fef-a148-a06913542f40`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- diantennary complex type (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A complex N-glycan that has two branches attached to the non-reducing terminal Man residues of the trimannosyl core. . Each branch is initiated with a GlcNAc and can be exteneded or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues. [CHEBI:156251] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- N-linked glycans with a complex-type structure characterized by two antennae (branches) extending from the core. These glycans are commonly found on glycoproteins and are known for their specific lectin binding properties. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch29/#:~:text=In%20contrast%2C%20many%20highly%20branched,the%20equilibrium%20binding%20constants (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- The crystal structure of Pterocarpus angolensis lectin is determined in its ligand-free state, in complex with the fucosylated biantennary complex type decasaccharide NA2F, and in complex with a series of smaller oligosaccharide constituents of NA2F.[PMID:16704415] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 34 references

### biantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000022` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:abc37e9a-7363-5440-87f1-1e620ed70a9a`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- diantennary (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Type of N-linked glycan in which two antenna are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). Each antenna can be exteneded or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues. [Essentials of Glycobiology:Glossary] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Biantennary N-glycans are N-linked glycans characterized by two antennae (branches) extending from the core pentasaccharide structure. These branches can be further modified by the addition of sugars such as galactose, sialic acid, or fucose. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

### bisected complex-type N-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:c453bc0a-a177-5fa0-b20d-23636ce0200e`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A complex-type N-glycan structure that contains a bisecting N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) residue attached via β1-4 linkage to the core mannose of N-glycans. This modification is typically introduced by the enzyme MGAT3. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=L-PHA%20binding%20is%20dramatically,samples%20differ%20in%20glycosylation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### bisecting GlcNAc

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000024` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:69988744-7296-5f26-8c2b-7a2cf6cd3ad3`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- bisecting N-acetylglucosamine (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- β1-4-GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- The bisecting GlcNAc is a modification of hybrid or complex N-linked glycans in which a single GlcNAc is added to the 3 position of the b-linked Man found in the trimannosyl core structure. The modification is catalyzed by beta 1,4-N-acetylglucosaminyltransferase III (GlcNAcT-III) or MGAT3 (E.C. 2.4.1.144). The addition of a bisecting GlcNAc on a hybrid or trimmed hybrid glycan prevents the subsequent action of N-glycan branching glycosyltransferases GlcNAcT-IV and GlcNAcT-V, but GlcNAcT3 can add a bisecting GlcNAc to the products of these enzymes. [PMID: 22476631] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- A β1-4-linked N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) residue attached to the core mannose of N-glycans, formed by the action of N-acetylglucosaminyltransferase III (MGAT3). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- The bisecting GlcNAc of N-glycans regulates cellular signaling and tumor progression through modulating N-glycan/galectin interactions.[PMID: 22476631]Inhibits growth factor signaling and retards mammary tumor progression[PMID: 20395209]Integrin-mediated cell adhesion [PMID: 20816221]Important in fertilization and fetal development, neuritogenesis, immune tolerance, immunoglobulin G (IgG) (Source:  32719771)

**Disease Associations:**
- mammary tumor. (Source:  20395209)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Increased%20expression%20of%20FUT8%20that,depend%20on%20tissue%20context (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch28/#:~:text=It%20is%20also%20noteworthy%20that%20viruses,evolved%20by%20convergent%20evolution (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- The presence of a bisecting GlcNAc and the occurrence of alpha 2-6-linked Neu5Ac in the most abundant N-glycans, are new features for hCG-beta.[PMID:1374031] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 51 references

### bisecting α1-4GlcNAc

**Term UUID:** `GSD:404bf970-2534-5f2f-86e1-746f0302e08a`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- α1-4GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A structural feature in certain fungal N-glycans where an N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) is attached in an α1-4 linkage to the β-mannose core, distinct from the common β1-4-linked bisecting GlcNAc in mammals. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### CA19-9

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d5606d4d-5018-4a71-997a-6b29f4187a99`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- carbohydrate antigen 19-9 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Related Synonyms:**
- sialyl Lewis a (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Carbohydratae Antigen (CA) (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A tumor-associated carbohydrate antigen identical to the sialyl Lewis a (sLeᵃ) tetrasaccharide [Neu5Acα2-3Galβ1-3(Fucα1-4)GlcNAc]. It functions as an E-selectin ligand and serves as the principal serum biomarker for pancreatic and gastrointestinal adenocarcinomas, where it is widely used for diagnostic and prognostic purposes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch46/#:~:text=Some%20of%20these%20changes%20have%20been,consisting%20predominantly%20of%20mucins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### CD77

**Term UUID:** `GSD:2721b555-911d-4079-a026-e761a752deb8`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Related Synonyms:**
- Gb3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Cluster of Differentiation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- CD77 is a neutral glycosphingolipid consisting of Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer, also known as globotriaosylceramide (Gb3). It serves as a tumor-associated carbohydrate antigen, being highly enriched in certain cancers such as Burkitt’s lymphoma, and is recognized by specific monoclonal antibodies. As a component of lipid raft microdomains, CD77 influences cell signaling and can modulate tumor cell growth and invasion. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Many%20%E2%80%9Ctumor-specific%E2%80%9D%20monoclonal,signaling%20by%20numerous%20receptors (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### chitobiose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:8756b00b-9dcc-514c-a965-d339f8ec93b2`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- chito-motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Chitobiose is a disaccharide composed of two β(1→4)-linked N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) units, forming the core structure of N-glycans. In some biological contexts, this chitobiose core can be elongated by additional monosaccharides, particularly in cases involving defects in mannosyltransferase activity or in certain parasitic organisms, resulting in extended chitobiose motifs. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch39/#:~:text=NGLY1%20recognizes%20only%20misfolded%20or,%28ER%29%2C%20cytoplasm%2C%20and%20lysosomes (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=In%20recent%20years%2C%20it%20has%20been%20shown,have%20similar%20defects%20%28Chapter%2049%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=The%20cattle%20parasite%20D.%20viviparous,Examples%20of%20nematode%20glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GM1b (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Related Synonyms:**
- GM1b (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- A member of the class of neuraminic acids that is neuraminic acid attached in sequence to β-D-galactopyranosyl, 2-acetamido-2-deoxy-β-D-galactopyranosyl, β-D-galactopyranosyl, and β-D-glucoopyranose units by (2→3), (1→3), (1→4), and (1→4) glycosidic linkages, respectively. It corresponds to the carbohydrate portion of ganglioside GM1b.[CHEBI:145580] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Disease Associations:**
- Influenza (Source: 92)

**Evidence:**
- Macrophage ganglioside patterns dramatically increase in complexity when murine peritoneal macrophages are stimulated in vivo with the appearance of the sialidase-sensitive monosialoganglioside GM1b (cisGM1) as a major component.[PMID:9455923] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### complex ganglioside

**Term UUID:** `GSD:83407b75-f351-5941-a80f-f5f3e662a358`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Complex gangliosides are gangliosides with multiple glycan extensions beyond the simple monosialylated core, often containing additional sialic acid and N-acetylgalactosamine residues. They are synthesized by the sequential action of sialyltransferases and N-acetylgalactosaminyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=The%20patients%E2%80%99%20plasma%20GSLs%20are,the%20progressive%20gait%20disorder (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### complex glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:918a1972-40ae-5a6e-9066-0b3b1c6fc5cf`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- complex oligosaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Complex glycans are oligosaccharides with branched and extended structures, typically referring to mature N-glycans or O-glycans that have undergone extensive processing and addition of various sugars beyond the core. These structures often feature complex sugar chains, such as sialylated, sulfated, glucuronylated, or zwitterionic modifications, and include both complex N-glycans and other complex-type glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=Consumption%20of%20these%20plants%20by,vs.%20%CE%B1-mannosidase%29%20does%20not%20exist (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=Although%20it%20was%20once%20thought%20that,in%20Drosophila%20and%20other%20insects (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=GlcNAcT-I%20catalyzes%20the%20first,have%20not%20been%20characterized (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### complex glycolipid

**Term UUID:** `GSD:01252e39-1f93-59e0-a64c-0655bfa4d620`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Complex glycolipids are glycolipids that possess oligosaccharide chains longer or more branched than those found in simple mono- or disaccharide glycolipids. They are synthesized from precursors like glucosylceramide through additional glycosylation steps. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=Because%20cells%20take%20up,is%20missing%20%28Chapter%2044%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### complex O-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:ebb26565-ed41-5f38-b9a5-a436515b2e86`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Complex O-glycans are O-glycans with extended and branched structures, typically formed by the sequential addition of monosaccharides to an initial N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) linked to serine or threonine residues of proteins. These glycans are highly diverse and are commonly found on mucins and other glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch08/#:~:text=The%20baculovirus%20enzyme%20ecdysteroid,in%20their%20symbiosis%20and%20virulence (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### complex-type N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000045` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:8898cfbc-b227-5381-aa3b-2d6e8f5c855c`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- complex N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- complex N-linked glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- complex-type glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Type of N-linked glycan in which at least two antenna are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). Each antenna can be exteneded or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Complex-type N-glycans are a class of N-linked glycans characterized by the addition of N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) to the core mannose residues, followed by further branching and elongation with sugars such as galactose, sialic acid, and fucose. These glycans are typically multiantennary and highly branched, formed after mannose trimming in the Golgi apparatus and subsequent action of glycosyltransferases. Their biosynthesis involves full processing of the GlcNAcMan5GlcNAc2 intermediate, and they are commonly found on glycoproteins in higher eukaryotes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- Involved in protein stability. (Source: 12417032)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch09/#:~:text=Paucimannose%20glycans%20are%20also,GlcNAc-TIII%20%28MGAT3%29%20%28Figure%209.5%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch49/#:~:text=Similarly%2C%20one%20of%20the%20first,cause%20an%20obvious%20growth%20phenotype (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=Complex%20and%20hybrid-type%20N-glycans,the%20mammalian%20immune%20system (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch27/#:~:text=Deletion%20of%20both%20%CE%B1-mannosidases,lack%20the%20same%20enzyme%20%28Chapter%2045%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch30/#:~:text=Ideally%2C%20the%20saturation%20effect,of%20the%20virus%20to%20target%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### Forssman glycolipid

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000069` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G80062GG`

![Forssman glycolipid](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G80062GG)

- **Image URL:** [G80062GG](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G80062GG)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G80062GG)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:2436d14a-7e6b-4999-b356-0d6bb0c091d9`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Forssman antigen (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Globopentosylceramide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- A linear amino pentasaccharide comprising N-acetyl-α-D-galactosamine, N-acetyl-β-D-galactosamine, α-D-galactose, β-D-galactose and β-D-glucose residues linked sequentially (1→3), (1→3), (1→4) and (1→4).[CHEBI:85789] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GalNAc(a1-3)GalNAc(b1-3)Gal(a1-4)Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- an antigenic marker for a major subpopulation of macrophages from murine spleen and peripheral lymph nodes (Source: 3495590)

**Disease Associations:**
- Pyelonephritis (Source: 86)
- Acute cystitis (Source: 86)

**Evidence:**
- Analysis of metabolically labeled glycolipids released into the apical and basal culture medium, either as shed membrane vesicles or in budding viruses, also demonstrated the presence of the Forssman glycolipid on both apical and basolateral membranes of polarized cells.[PMID:3040119] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 32 references
- [PMID:10431785](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10431785/)
- [PMID:10460829](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10460829/)
- [PMID:10506200](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10506200/)
- [PMID:11214737](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11214737/)
- [PMID:1138893](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1138893/)
- [PMID:14573676](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/14573676/)
- [PMID:16133832](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/16133832/)
- [PMID:1747956](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1747956/)
- [PMID:1868069](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1868069/)
- [PMID:1933935](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1933935/)
- *...and 22 more publications*

**Database Cross-References:**
- [CHEBI:85789](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/searchId.do?chebiId=CHEBI:85789)
- [CID:91825690](https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/compound/91825690)
- [GlycoEpitope:EP0037](https://glycoepitope.jp/)
- [GlycoMotif:GGM.000084](https://glycomotif.org/)
- KEGG:G00095
- SugarBind_Ligand:86

### fucosylated asialo-biantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000072` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d38f6ccf-3575-4e17-b5db-17d3abc498bb`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- fucosylated asialo-diantennary (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- Describing a biantennary oligosaccharide that has no sialic acid component but has fucose. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- We found that approximately 63% of the total N-linked oligosaccharides were of the complex type, with bisialo-biantennary (28%), asialo-biantennary (7%), fucosylated bisialo-biantennary (25%), and fucosylated asialo-biantennary (3%) oligosaccharides representing the major complex-type carbohydrate species.[PMID:2065054] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 2 references
- [PMID:17013932](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17013932/)
- [PMID:2065054](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/2065054/)

---

### fucosylated biantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000073` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:73b79304-0089-48fc-afd4-82b84a0c1e53`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- fucosylated bisialo-diantennary (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- a biantennary glycan with two GlcNAc branches linked to the core and the addition of fucose. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- The profiles of the PSA N-glycans from the free and complexed molecules were quite similar to each other and consisted of fucosylated biantennary oligosaccharides as the major class.[PMID:17956937] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 3 references
- [PMID:17956937](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17956937/)
- [PMID:1933856](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1933856/)
- [PMID:30525457](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30525457/)

### fucosylated glycans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:48b33e4c-bedf-5af1-ba2a-8730ba59a999`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Fucosylated glycans are glycans that contain fucose residues, typically attached through α1-2, α1-3, or α1-4 linkages. These structures play important roles in biological processes such as host-pathogen interactions and cell signaling. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch03/#:~:text=As%20molecules%2C%20they%20are%20generally,little%20or%20none%20of%20this%20compound (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch05/#:~:text=It%20is%20especially%20difficult%20to,pools%20and%20glycosyltransferases (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=There%20is%20a%20relatively%20high,of%20these%20abundant%20glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch37/#:~:text=As%20such%2C%20these%20toxins%20function%20by,on%20the%20surface%20of%20host%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### fucosylated lacdiNAc

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000075` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G02240AO`

![fucosylated lacdiNAc](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G02240AO)

- **Image URL:** [G02240AO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G02240AO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G02240AO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b7812d76-e661-481a-bc07-21470f1c5f7f`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- (GalNAc)1 (GlcNAc)1 (LFuc)1 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Fucosylated lacdiNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- LDN-F (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- LDNF (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- fucosylated LacdiNAc (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Definition:**
- An amino trisaccharide comprising an acetamido-β-D-galactose and an α-L-fucose attached to an acetamidoglucose by 1→4 and 1→3 linkages, respectively.[CHEBI:61864] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GalNAc(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(?1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**
- To date, oligomeric LDN and oligomeric fucosylated LDN (LDNF) have been found only on N-glycans from mammalian cells engineered to express Caenorhabditis elegansbeta4-GalNAc transferase and human alpha3-fucosyltransferase IX [Z. S. Kawar et al. (2005) J Biol Chem280, 12810-12819]. [PMID:16403022] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### fucosylated N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000076` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:513ef5dc-3e7b-5d21-9757-fd24642c2e46`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- The fucose from GDP-fucose is transferred to N-linked glycans. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- N-glycans that are modified by the addition of fucose residues. The presence of fucose can influence various biological processes, including neural development and function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Disease Associations:**
- pancreatic cancer. (Source:  26869352)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch41/#:~:text=Mutant%20mice%20lacking%20some%20complex,chapters%20referred%20to%20above (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Haptoglobin , an acute phase protein , has four potential N-glycosylation sites , although it remains unknown which site is responsible for the change in fucosylated N-glycans.[PMID:18214858] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 5 references
- [PMID:18214858](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18214858/)
- [PMID:26869352](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26869352/)
- [PMID:29790113](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29790113/)
- [PMID:31141660](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31141660/)
- [PMID:31931062](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31931062/)

### fucosylated tetraantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000070` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:5b125ad0-374d-4de2-96dd-0c3ac0133556`

**Definition:**
- A tetraantennary glycan with four GlcNAc branches linked to the core and the addition of fucose. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- The two major N-linked oligosaccharides of Namalwa EPO were fucose-containing tetraantennary and fucose-containing triantennary structures.[PMID:2550193] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 1 references

### fucosylated triantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000077` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b209bf99-fab6-4875-aaad-65c95d126517`

**Definition:**
- A triantennary glycan with three GlcNAc branches linked to the core and the addition of fucose. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Biological Functions:**
- appears in human alpha-fetoprotein (AFP). (Source:  7679920)

**Disease Associations:**
- neoplastic diseases of the liver (Source:  7679920 )
- hepatocellular carcinoma[PMID: 7679920]. (Source: unknown)

**Evidence:**
- Asn184 is only partially glycosylated and substituted by fucosylated triantennary and small amounts of diantennary N-acetyllactosaminic glycans.[PMID:2513186] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### Fucα1-2Gal

**Term UUID:** `GSD:04968aef-3b4e-5f07-8352-79ac445ab345`

**Classification:**
- IUPAC String (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A disaccharide composed of fucose linked α1-2 to galactose. This motif is commonly found as a terminal structure on host epithelial glycoconjugates, with its expression regulated by bacterial exposure and fucosyltransferase activity. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

- **Image URL:** [G36191CD](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G36191CD)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:bf0e78e7-7656-5f66-8677-a96d59743cfe`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- G0 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Related Synonyms:**
- A2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GLYGEN_CURATORS_NCOMPO)

**Classification:**
- IgG notation (Source: GLYGEN_CURATORS_NCOMPO)
- N-Glycan Code (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A complex-type N-glycan that lacks galactose residues on its antennae (agalactosylated), typically featuring a core Man3GlcNAc2 structure with two terminal GlcNAc residues. This structure is commonly found on glycoproteins and is recognized by mannose-binding proteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- afucosylated agalactosylated biantennary N-glycan (Source: GLYGEN_CURATORS_NCOMPO)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch46/#:~:text=Other%20structural%20features%20are%20also,interactions%20with%20Fc%20receptors (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### galabiose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:a2c78d58-1d1e-5488-972e-60788bd92662`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Galα1-4Gal determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Galα1-4Gal moiety (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Galabiose is a disaccharide composed of two galactose residues linked by an alpha1-4 glycosidic bond (Galα1-4Gal). It serves as a recognition motif for certain bacterial toxins and adhesins, and is found in glycolipids and glycoproteins, including as part of glycosphingolipids where it can be linked to ceramide. Galabiose-containing structures are present in the large intestine epithelium and function as receptors for specific pathogens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch37/#:~:text=There%2C%20the%20enzymatic%20A1%20chain,receptor%20Gb3%20results%20in%20cell%20death (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=This%20syndrome%20is%20distinct%20from%20CAD,following%20infection%20in%20utero (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch37/#:~:text=The%20columnar%20epithelium%20that%20lines,further%20illustrated%20by%20E.%20coli%20K99 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch37/#:~:text=Mice%20lacking%20the%20gene%20encoding%20the,Thus%2C%20Bacterioides%2C%20Clostridium%2C%20E (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch37/#:~:text=The%20columnar%20epithelium%20that%20lines,further%20illustrated%20by%20E.%20coli%20K99 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### galactolipid

**Term UUID:** `GSD:a92a3278-eedb-5329-b627-fe354524b4f1`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Galactolipids are a class of glycolipids characterized by one or more galactose residues attached to a lipid moiety. They are commonly found in plant membranes and play important roles in membrane structure and function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=The%20synthesis%20of%20these,for%20normal%20photosynthesis%20to%20occur (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- This tetrasaccharide motif consists of galactose β1-3 linked to N-acetylglucosamine, which is β1-3 linked to galactose, itself β1-4 linked to N-acetylglucosamine. It is recognized by TRA-1-60 and TRA-1-81 antibodies as part of keratan sulfate–related antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### ganglio-series glycosphingolipid

**Term UUID:** `GSD:cac8e53d-b210-51f2-8b22-4d0f32b77326`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- ganglio-series (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Ganglio-series glycosphingolipids are a class of glycosphingolipids defined by a specific core oligosaccharide structure (Galβ1-3GalNAcβ1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer), which can be further extended and sialylated to form gangliosides. These molecules are predominant in the mammalian brain and are classified based on the number of sialic acid residues and their migration order on thin-layer chromatography, such as GM (mono), GD (di), and GT (tri) series. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch54/#:~:text=Glycosyltransferase-mediated,was%20employed%20twice%20%28Figure%2054.3%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=GSL%20series%20are%20expressed%20in,ganglio-series%20neutral%20sugar%20core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=For%20example%2C%20in%20the%20widely%20used,as%20examples%20are%20major%20brain%20GSLs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### ganglioside

**Term UUID:** `GSD:7a810b44-8bb6-56ba-b76c-339d66b301d7`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Gangliosides are glycosphingolipids (glycolipids) characterized by one or more sialic acid residues linked to an oligosaccharide chain, which is attached to a ceramide lipid. As a subclass of sialylated glycosphingolipids, they are abundant in the nervous system, particularly the brain, and function as important cell surface molecules involved in cell signaling, recognition, and as receptors for toxins and viruses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch07/#:~:text=Another%20striking%20example%20is%20the,membrane%20microdomains%20%28Chapter%2011%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch07/#:~:text=Although%20the%20precise%20mechanisms%20of,affected%20by%20glycosylation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch44/#:~:text=When%20wild-type%20mice%20were%20treated,but%20prevented%20macrocephaly (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch04/#:~:text=Taken%20together%2C%20evidence%20indicates,topology%20of%20the%20claimed%20structures (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch07/#:~:text=There%20are%20many%20receptors%20whose,to%20these%20functions%20of%20glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### Glc3Man9GlcNAc2

**Term UUID:** `GSD:cc06bb89-924b-5d6d-a843-baca062dd6a4`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Composition (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Glc3Man9GlcNAc2 is a fully assembled N-glycan precursor oligosaccharide consisting of three glucose, nine mannose, and two N-acetylglucosamine residues. It is transferred en bloc to nascent proteins, covalently linked to asparagine, during N-glycosylation in eukaryotes, representing the initial glycan attached to glycoproteins before further processing in the endoplasmic reticulum and Golgi apparatus. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch09/#:~:text=This%2014-sugar%20glycan%20is,in%20the%20process%20%28Figure%209.3%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### glycosphingolipids

**Term UUID:** `GSD:aaadcfba-0977-5dd9-965d-40aa49d81920`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GSLs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Glycosphingolipids (GSLs) are a subclass of glycolipids composed of a glycan (oligosaccharide) linked to a ceramide lipid moiety. They are major components of animal cell membranes, synthesized by the stepwise addition of sugars to ceramide, and are classified based on their glycan structures. GSLs play important roles in membrane structure, cell–cell recognition, and signal transduction. They are degraded in lysosomes, and defects in their catabolism can result in pathological accumulation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=Ronald%20L.%20Schnaar%2C%20Roger%20Sandhoff%2C,as%20membrane%20anchors%20%28Chapter%2012%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=Glycosphingolipids%20%28GSLs%29%20are,and%20adsorption%20chromatographies (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=elegans%20is%20similar%20to%20the%20chains,%28GSLs%29%20%28Chapter%2011%29%20in%20C (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### GPI glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:6541a9ff-3d21-5b09-8297-2ae4b63eb6c4`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GPI anchor (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Manα1-2Manα1-6Manα1-4GlcNα1-6Inositolphospholipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- glycosylphosphatidylinositol (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- GPI glycans are the glycan (oligosaccharide) portion of glycosylphosphatidylinositol (GPI) anchors, which are glycolipid structures that tether proteins to the outer leaflet of the plasma membrane. The core GPI glycan consists of a conserved sequence, typically including mannose and glucosamine residues linked to myo-inositol phospholipid, and serves as a scaffold for further modifications and protein attachment. GPI glycans can vary between species and tissues, contributing to the diversity of GPI-anchored proteins, and may include additional side chains. In some organisms, the lipid component is inositolphosphoceramide. The GPI glycan is preassembled before protein attachment and is essential for the function of GPI anchors. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=Thus%2C%20primary%20control%20occurs%20at,significance%20remains%20unknown (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=Once%20labeled%20and%20dephosphorylated,of%20a%20cell-free%20system%20in%20T.%20brucei (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch49/#:~:text=Loss%20of%20GPI-anchor%20biosynthesis,of%20the%20GPI%20anchor%20to%20protein (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=For%20example%2C%20neural%20cell%20adhesion,proteoglycans%29%20moiety%20%28Chapter%2017%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=Sneha%20Sudha%20Komath%2C%20Morihisa,type-I%20integral%20membrane%20proteins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- An amino trisaccharide consisting of a 3-O-sulfo-β-D-glucuronic acid residue, a D-galactosyl residue and an N-acetyl-D-glucosamine residue (at the reducing end) in a linear sequence.[CHEBI:60322] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- HNK-1 is a carbohydrate epitope, specifically 3-sulfoglucuronyl-galactose, found on various glycans, including those present on glycosphingolipids. Glycosphingolipids that display the HNK-1 epitope have this distinctive glycan structure on their glycan moiety. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Disease Associations:**
- schizophrenia-like psychosis (Source: EP0001)
- neuropathy (Source: EP0001)
- exfoliation syndrome (Source: EP0001)
- secondary cataract (Source: EP0001)

### hybrid N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000110` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G16828VN`

![hybrid N-glycan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G16828VN)

- **Image URL:** [G16828VN](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G16828VN)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G16828VN)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d4bdcdc4-b702-5263-b380-9175249b1ed6`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- hybrid-type N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- hybrid-type glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- An N-linked glycan in which the Manα1-6 arm of the trimannosyl core Manα1-3(Manα1-6)Manβ1-4GlcNAcβ1–4GlcNAcβ1–Asn-X-Ser/Thr is substituted by one or more additional Man residues and the Manα1-3 arm is substituted by a GlcNAc residue added by GlcNAcT1. The GlcNAc linked to the a3 Man can be extended in the same manner as any complex glycan antenna. The trimannosyl core can also be core fucosylated and/or bisected by the addition of a GlcNAc to the 3 position of the α-linked Man residue.[Essentials of Glycobiology:Chapter 9] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Hybrid N-glycans are a class of N-linked glycans that display features of both oligomannose and complex N-glycans. They are characterized by having one branch (antenna) that retains mannose residues, while the other branch is extended with N-acetylglucosamine and other complex sugars. These structures are biosynthesized when the GlcNAcMan5GlcNAc2 intermediate produced by MGAT1 is not fully processed by α-mannosidase II, resulting in glycans that are intermediates between oligomannose and complex N-glycans. Hybrid N-glycans can be further modified in the Golgi apparatus. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch27/#:~:text=In%20most%20cases%2C%20these%20mutations,of%20hybrid%20and%20complex%20N-glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch44/#:~:text=Cathepsin%20A%20protects,hybrid%20and%20complex-type%20N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch09/#:~:text=It%20is%20important%20to%20note%20that,and%20stem%20cell%20development (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=In%20the%20trans-Golgi%2C%20%CE%B1-Fucf%20may%20be,or%20transported%20to%20the%20vacuoles (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch29/#:~:text=Such%20an%20approach%20has%20been,N-glycans%20will%20not%20bind (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### LacdiNAc

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000115` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G31736BK`

![LacdiNAc](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G31736BK)

- **Image URL:** [G31736BK](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G31736BK)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G31736BK)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:cea1d668-1fe6-50d0-9982-477ee729a810`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GalNAcβ1-4GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- GalNAcβ1-4GlcNAcβ1- (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- LDN (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- LacdiNAc (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)
- N-acetylgalactosamine β1-4 N-acetylglucosamine (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- An amino disaccharide consisting of N-acetylglucosamine having an N-acetylgalactosaminyl resiude attached at the 4-position via a β-linkage.[CHEBI:61190] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- LacdiNAc (N-acetylgalactosamine β1-4 N-acetylglucosamine, GalNAcβ1-4GlcNAc) is a disaccharide motif consisting of N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) β1-4 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc). It is commonly found as an outer structure in some invertebrate glycans and as a motif in N-glycans, with its presence determined by specific glycosyltransferases in lineage-specific glycosylation pathways. LacdiNAc is a glycan motif found in various taxa. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GalNAc(b1-4)GlcNAc(?1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- Has a role of epitope. (Source: 61190)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=R-type%20lectins%20are%20present%20in,of%20cultured%20mammalian%20tumor%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch20/#:~:text=In%20some%20taxa%2C%20such%20as%20mollusks%2C%20an,monosaccharide%20building%20blocks (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch20/#:~:text=In%20contrast%2C%20trimming%20to,expression%20of%20processing%20enzymes (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- We previously showed that the expression level of the LacdiNAc group in N-glycans decreases dramatically during the progression of human breast cancer.[PMID:25003135] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 47 references

### lacto-series glycosphingolipids

**Term UUID:** `GSD:6b96f33b-d7b7-5e34-ad41-d3b621495dff`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- lacto-series (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Lacto-series glycosphingolipids are a class of glycosphingolipids defined by a core structure containing N-acetyllactosamine (LacNAc) repeats. These glycolipids are especially prominent in secretory organs and play important roles in cellular recognition and membrane structure. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=GSL%20series%20are%20expressed%20in,ganglio-series%20neutral%20sugar%20core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### LcGg4

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000118` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G62554JH`

![LcGg4](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G62554JH)

- **Image URL:** [G62554JH](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G62554JH)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G62554JH)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:761b72f4-76a5-4cab-b30b-ade71d188c64`

**Definition:**
- N-[(2S,3R,4R,5R,6R)-2-[(2R,3S,4R,5R,6S)-4-[(2S,3R,4R,5S,6R)-3-Acetamido-4,5-dihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-2-yl]oxy-5-hydroxy-2-(hydroxymethyl)-6-[(2R,3S,4R,5R,6R)-4,5,6-trihydroxy-2-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-3-yl]oxyoxan-3-yl]oxy-4,5-dihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-3-yl]acetamide is an oligosaccharide.[CHEBI:152521] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- Murine leukemia cells (M1), in their undifferentiated state, have been characterized by the presence of cancer-associated lactoganglio-series glycolipids, one of which was identified as lactogangliotetraosylceramide (LcGg4) having a novel branching at the II-Gal of lactosylceramide through GlcNAc beta 1----3 and GalNAc beta 1----4 linkage, as shown below (Kannagi, R., Levery, S.B., and Hakomori, S. (1984) J. Biol. Chem., 259, 8444-8451): GalNAc beta 1----4 Gal beta 1----4Glc beta 1----1Cer GlcNac beta 1----3 [PMID: 3805024] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### lipid A core oligosaccharide

**Term UUID:** `GSD:29cf4976-eff8-5c90-b2ce-05ee2f1e908a`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- The lipid A-core is a glycan-lipid structure in which lipid A is covalently linked to the core oligosaccharide region of lipopolysaccharide (LPS). This structure serves as a substrate for transport and further modification in Gram-negative bacteria. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch21/#:~:text=The%20formation%20of%20Kdo2%E2%80%93lipid%20A%20is,of%20two%20main%20synthetic%20strategies (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch21/#:~:text=The%20assembly%20of%20O-PS%20occurs,appropriate%20for%20their%20function%28s%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### lipo-chitooligosaccharide

**Term UUID:** `GSD:97780e7f-7a97-52e0-bcef-62e033517882`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- LCO (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- lipochitooligosaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Lipo-chitooligosaccharides (LCOs) are a class of glycolipids consisting of a chitin-derived oligosaccharide backbone linked to a fatty acid. They are produced by bacterial and fungal symbionts and are recognized by plant receptors, triggering various biological responses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch07/#:~:text=However%2C%20even%20the%20most%20precise,and%20structure-dependent%20manner (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### lipoglycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:5ce320b8-ced2-5597-9134-b409afd7632f`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Lipoglycans are a broad class of glycoconjugates in which a glycan, such as an oligosaccharide or polysaccharide, is covalently linked to a lipid moiety. These molecules contribute to cell membrane structure and function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch22/#:~:text=Methanosarcina%20species%20can%20further,number%20of%20proteins%20%28Figure%2022.1%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### lipopeptidophosphoglycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:755bd8b9-fb88-5107-b53c-4a9fa6a0f664`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- LPPG (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A complex glycoconjugate composed of lipid, peptide, and phosphoglycan components, as exemplified by the lipopeptidophosphoglycan (LPPG) found in Entamoeba histolytica. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch43/#:~:text=Leishmania%20LPG%20and%20LPPG%20are,inflammatory%20responses (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### lipophosphoglycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:dea28036-c3f0-562f-9196-0d68504e1445`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- LPG (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Lipophosphoglycan (LPPG) is a major surface glycoconjugate of Leishmania parasites, consisting of a phosphoglycan chain linked to a lipid anchor. It functions as a virulence factor and plays a key role in parasite biology. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch43/#:~:text=Leishmania%20LPG%20and%20LPPG%20are,inflammatory%20responses (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch43/#:~:text=Leishmania%20LPG%20and%20LPPG%20are,inflammatory%20responses (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### lipopolysaccharide

**Term UUID:** `GSD:ce35d9a0-c346-5335-8128-5430003359c9`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- LPS (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Lipopolysaccharide is a major glycolipid component of the outer membrane of Gram-negative bacteria, composed of lipid A, a core oligosaccharide, and an O-antigen polysaccharide. It acts as a potent antigen and pathogen-associated molecular pattern (PAMP). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch03/#:~:text=LPSs%20carry%20long%20polysaccharides,diverse%20set%20of%20polymer%20structures (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch03/#:~:text=Some%20polymers%20are%20known%20under,affect%20polysaccharide%20properties (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch03/#:~:text=The%20interactions%20of%20bacteria%20with,leaflet%20of%20the%20outer%20membrane (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch05/#:~:text=Deoxyhexoses%20are%20often,CDP-glucose%20dehydratase (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=Sias%20in%20capsular%20polysaccharides,to%20Sia%20pathways%20of%20vertebrates (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### M7BC

**Term UUID:** `GSD:649ec44c-8ca8-51fc-ab09-791ddb37fde2`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Code (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Oxford notation (Source: GLYGEN_CURATORS_NCOMPO)

**Description:**
- M7BC is a specific isomer of the Man7 (M7) high-mannose N-glycan. It is characterized by the absence of residues i and k compared to M8B, resulting in the exposure of an α1–6-linked mannose (residue j). M7BC is generated from M8B through the enzymatic action of EDEM1 and EDEM3. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- afucosylated high-mannose (7) N-glycan, non-mannosylated branch B and C (Source: GLYGEN_CURATORS_NCOMPO)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch39/#:~:text=Mammalian%20cells%20have%2C%20in%20addition,Yos9p%20in%20Saccharomyces%20cerevisiae%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch39/#:~:text=The%20M7BC%20isomer%20exposes%20an%20%CE%B11%E2%80%936Man,protein%20called%20Htm1p%20are%20present (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:3b8d8834-2436-5048-9e58-0a0c4ed97585`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Code (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Oxford notation (Source: GLYGEN_CURATORS_NCOMPO)

**Description:**
- M8B is a specific isomer of the Man8 (M8) high-mannose N-glycan. It is generated from Man9 (M9) through demannosylation, a process mainly catalyzed by the enzyme EDEM2 in the endoplasmic reticulum. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- afucosylated high-mannose (8) N-glycan, non-mannosylated branch B (Source: GLYGEN_CURATORS_NCOMPO)

### Man-6-P-containing N-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:dc08638c-1152-5e27-adb4-2e805b91dffc`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- M6P-containing glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- N-glycans that contain mannose-6-phosphate (Man-6-P) residues. These glycans were first identified on lysosomal enzymes and are essential for proper lysosomal trafficking, serving as recognition markers for targeting enzymes to lysosomes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch07/#:~:text=These%20glycans%20have%20potent%20effects,for%20different%20functional%20roles (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### monoantennary N-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:6d8d4be9-d746-5d48-9da6-ca1f0eb3b919`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A monoantennary N-glycan is an N-glycan structure characterized by having a single antenna (branch) extending from the core. This type of glycan is often produced in engineered CHO cells, is typically unsialylated, and features exposed galactose acceptor sites. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=The%20process%20is%20in%20use%20with,the%20initiation%20of%20O-GalNAc%20glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:9dbd00b1-daa1-5dd7-8643-cfc47dc7f67e`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A high-mannose-type N-glycan containing a single glucose residue. This structure is recognized by calnexin and calreticulin in the endoplasmic reticulum and plays a key role in the glycoprotein folding quality control system. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

### mucin-type O-glycans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:07f53917-abb0-5512-9eb2-b97850950eee`

**Related Synonyms:**
- mucin-type O-glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Mucin-type O-glycans (O-GalNAc glycans) are a class of O-linked glycans that begin with the attachment of N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) to serine or threonine residues on proteins. They are commonly found on mucins and other glycoproteins, sharing a core GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr structure that can be further extended or modified by additional sugars. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=Living%20systems%20have%20evidently,peptide%20and%20UDP-GalNAc%20as%20donor (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=Inka%20Brockhausen%2C%20Hans%20H.%20Wandall%2C,and%20Gal%20and%20GlcNAc%20by%20sulfation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch23/#:~:text=S.%20cerevisiae%20contributed%20to,found%20in%20vertebrates.%20However%2C%20S (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch27/#:~:text=Indeed%2C%20developmental%20changes%20in,against%20microbial%20infection (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### N-acetyl GM2

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000128` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G79389NT`

![N-acetyl GM2](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G79389NT)

- **Image URL:** [G79389NT](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G79389NT)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G79389NT)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:acce63a2-f38e-4f65-b35a-2ab99054214a`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GM2 (Neu5Ac) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- GM2 / Neu5Ac (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Definition:**
- beta-D-GalpNAc-(1-&gt;4)-[alpha-Neup5Ac-(2-&gt;3)]-beta-D-Galp-(1-&gt;4)-beta-D-Glcp is a branched amino tetrasaccharide consisting of the linear sequence β-D-GalNAc-(1→4)-β-D-Gal-(1→4)-β-D-Glc having a Neu5Ac residue attached to the galactose via an α-(2→3) linkage. Corresponds to the carbohydrate portion of ganglioside GM2.[CHEBI:59220] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-3)[GalNAc(b1-4)]Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- The biological role played by a material entity when bound by a receptor of the adaptive immune system. Specific site on an antigen to which an antibody binds. (Source: 59220)

**Disease Associations:**
- lung adenocarcinoma (Source: EP0051)
- embryonal carcinoma (Source: EP0051)
- germ cell tumor (Source: EP0051)
- teratocarcinoma (Source: EP0051)
- squamous cell carcinoma (Source: EP0051)

**Evidence:**
- The appearance of the N-acetyl GM2 antigen correlated well with the degree of differentiation of the cancer cells in patients with squamous cell carcinoma and adenocarcinoma of the lung.[PMID:3167861] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 3 references
- [PMID:2153431](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/2153431/)
- [PMID:25673107](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/25673107/)
- [PMID:3167861](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/3167861/)

**Database Cross-References:**
- [CHEBI:59220](https://www.ebi.ac.uk/chebi/searchId.do?chebiId=CHEBI:59220)
- [CID:45266845](https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/compound/45266845)
- [GlycoEpitope:EP0051](https://glycoepitope.jp/)

### N-glycan core

**Term UUID:** `GSD:24d335a2-51a3-5232-96e6-2d60815f8e55`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- The N-glycan core is the conserved core structure (Manα1–6(Manα1–3)Manβ1–4GlcNAcβ1–4GlcNAcβ1–Asn) found in all N-linked glycans found on eukaryotic proteins. It is assembled during the process of N-glycosylation and is essential for the proper formation and function of glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=To%20date%2C%20relatively%20few%20mutations,fused%20lobes%20%28Figure%2026.2%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### N-glycans

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000131` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G78059CC`

![N-glycans](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G78059CC)

- **Image URL:** [G78059CC](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G78059CC)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G78059CC)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b6aa2621-651c-5dc5-af13-e3dc0b932d37`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- N-linked (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- N-linked oligosaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- asparagine–N-linked glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- The carbohydrate portion of a glycoprotein that has a glycan linked through the nitrogen of an asparagine side-chain.[CHEBI:59520] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- N-glycans are a class of glycans covalently attached to the asparagine residues of proteins via N-glycosidic bonds. They are characterized by a conserved core pentasaccharide structure and are commonly found in animals, but the N-glycosylation system is present across all domains of life. N-glycans are synthesized in the endoplasmic reticulum and Golgi apparatus, and their structures can be further elaborated with various monosaccharides such as GlcNAc, GalNAc, Gal, Man, Fuc, and sialic acid. These glycans play important roles in protein folding, stability, and cell signaling. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- N-glycans modulate the function of several cell surface proteins which involved in migration, adhesion and which are responsible for regulating myelination. (Source:  25151374)
- Affect transportation of the glycosylated proteins in the Golgi. (Source:  25151374)
- Affects cell-cell interaction, adhesion, expression, proper folding. (Source:  25151374)
- extracellular N-glycans act as key modulators of neuronal uptake of αacetyl [PMID: 31211781]. (Source: unknown)

**Disease Associations:**
- Rheumatoid arthritis (Source: 17392038)
- Type 1 Diabetes (Source: 29146600)
- Lung Cancer (Source:  23766031)

### sialoglycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:85de55d3-c7bc-5196-9eb1-80795c555e3e`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Sia-containing glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- sialic acid-containing glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Sialoglycans are glycans (oligosaccharides or polysaccharides) that contain one or more sialic acid residues, typically as terminal (outermost) sugars on structures such as N-glycans, O-glycans, glycosphingolipids, milk oligosaccharides, or glycosylphosphatidylinositol (GPI) anchors. These molecules play important roles in cell recognition, immune modulation, and interactions with pathogens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=Scientists%20monitoring%20influenza,and%20the%20structurally%20related%20E (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=Human%20mutations%20in%20Sialin%20cause,are%20rapidly%20excreted%20in%20the%20urine (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=The%20N-acetyl%20group%20facilitates,proteins%20and%20direct%20Sia%20function (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch54/#:~:text=After%20peracetylation%2C%20treatment,synthesis%20is%20very%20promising (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=Alternately%2C%20the%20same,type%2C%20tissue%20type%2C%20and%20species (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- *...and 16 more evidence entries*

- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### Siaα2-3Galβ1-4GlcNAc

**Term UUID:** `GSD:96329dbf-4f65-50bc-899b-539e25568d12`

**Description:**
- A trisaccharide glycan structure where sialic acid (Sia) is α2-3 linked to galactose (Gal), which is β1-4 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc). This structure can be formed by elongation of O-GlcNAc with β1,4Gal followed by sialylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- A trisaccharide glycan structure consisting of sialic acid (Sia) α2-6 linked to galactose (Gal), which is β1-4 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc). This motif is recognized by CD22 on B lymphocytes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- `Neu5Ac(a2-6)Gal(b1-4)GlcNAc(b1-3)Gal(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Mice%20lacking%20ST6GAL1%20show,members%2C%20and%20perhaps%20other%20lectins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Recently%2C%20a%20second%20%CE%B2-linked,to%20form%20Sia%CE%B12-3/6Gal%CE%B21-4GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### sulfatide

### terminal N-acetylgalactosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:cd483672-3ab7-5734-a40a-b489a133c301`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- terminal GalNAc residue (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residues located at the non-reducing end of glycan chains, commonly present on certain N-glycans and recognized by specific lectins and receptors. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch28/#:~:text=Once%20released%20from%20cells%2C,degradation%20of%20cellulose%20fibers (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Consequently%2C%20the%20biantennary,of%20the%20hormone%20by%20the%20pituitary (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### terminal N-acetylglucosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:7562f115-e4a2-5461-8cea-be68033775d0`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- exposed GlcNAc residues (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- terminal GlcNAc residue (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan feature where N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) is present at the nonreducing end of a glycan chain. This terminal GlcNAc can serve as an acceptor for galactosyltransferases and is recognized by certain lectins, playing a role in glycan recognition and modification. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch28/#:~:text=It%20is%20also%20noteworthy%20that%20viruses,evolved%20by%20convergent%20evolution (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=The%20purified%20rabbit%20AMR,the%20best%20characterized%20receptors (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch19/#:~:text=This%20seemingly%20simple%20carbohydrate,glycoconjugates%20%28Chapter%206%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=For%20example%2C%20the%20gene%20encoding%20a,glycans%20on%20their%20surface (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### terminal sialic acid

**Term UUID:** `GSD:7e50b61a-ea7e-5c9c-860e-a5296d3b2339`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Terminal Sia residue (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan feature characterized by the presence of sialic acid (Sia) residues at the nonreducing end of a glycan chain. These terminal sialic acids can be removed by mild acid treatment or further modified, and their removal exposes the underlying sugar residues. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=O-GalNAc%20glycans%2C%20as%20well%20as%20other,O-GalNAc%20glycans%20on%20a%20glycoprotein (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch09/#:~:text=Many%20of%20these%20structures%20are,of%20GNPTAB%20and%20GNPTG%29%20%28Figure%209.4%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch28/#:~:text=Like%20most%20glycoproteins,or%20GlcNAc%20residues%20from%20the%20blood (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### terminal β-linked N-acetylgalactosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:e16fd11e-688e-51f7-b405-dd0bb8b17879`

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan feature characterized by the presence of N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) at the nonreducing end of a glycan chain in a β-linkage. This terminal structure is recognized by specific lectins and receptors, including the asialoglycoprotein receptor and the AMR. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch28/#:~:text=Like%20most%20glycoproteins,or%20GlcNAc%20residues%20from%20the%20blood (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=The%20efficiency%20of%20the%20AMR%20in,normal%20without%20challenge (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### tetraantennary complex-type N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000165` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:283cf9cf-37cb-4610-ac96-5c27e7b5e032`

**Definition:**
- Type of N-linked glycan in which four antennae are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). The four antennae are initiated by addition of 2 GlcNAcs to the _3 Man (at the 2 and 4 positions) and 2 GlcNAcs to the _6 Man (at the 2 and 6 positions) of the trimannosyl core. Each antenna can be extended or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues.  [Essentials of Glycobiology:Glossary] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- A variety of high-mannose structures , as well as bi-, tri-, and tetraantennary complex-type carbohydrate , has been found, in relative amounts of 1-25% of the total oligosaccharides.[PMID:1899031] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### tetraantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000164` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:aee918cb-25f8-50b7-8174-0b8aee6bfdb1`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Type of N-linked glycan in which four antennae are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). The four antennae are initiated by addition of 2 GlcNAcs to the _3 Man (at the 2 and 4 positions) and 2 GlcNAcs to the _6 Man (at the 2 and 6 positions) of the trimannosyl core. Each antenna can be extended or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues.  [Essentials of Glycobiology:Glossary] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- A tetraantennary N-glycan is an N-linked glycan structure characterized by four antennae (branches) extending from the core. These branches often terminate in β-linked galactose (Gal) or N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residues and are frequently associated with increased sialylation. Tetra-antennary N-glycans are notable for their enhanced biological activity, such as in erythropoietin (EPO), and can exhibit high-affinity binding to certain receptors, including the rat AMR. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=Erythropoietin%20%28EPO%29%20is%20the%20most,activity%20in%20vivo%20nearly%20tenfold (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=The%20efficiency%20of%20the%20AMR%20in,normal%20without%20challenge (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- In contrast, myeloma IgE showed a higher abundance of triantennary and tetraantennary glycan structures and a low abundance of species with a bisecting N-acetylglucosamine.[PMID:24308486] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### triantennary complex-type N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000169` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:9598324c-4144-4a36-9363-8b23621950d1`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Triantennary complex-type (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- A complex N-glycan that has three branches attached to the non-reducing terminal Man residues of the trimannosyl core.[CHEBI:156252]  Each branch is initiated with a GlcNAc and can be exteneded or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### triantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000170` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:1471df12-b367-5f77-b47f-f8e31ae9707f`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- tri-antennary N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Type of N-linked glycan in which three antennae are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). The three antennae can be be initiated by addition of 2 GlcNAcs to the _3 Man (at the 2 and 4 positions) and 1 GlcNAc to the _6 Man (at the 2 position) of the trimannosyl core or 1 on the _3 and 2 on the _6 Man (at the 2 and 6 positions). Each antenna can be extended or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues.  [Essentials of Glycobiology:Glossary] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- A triantennary N-glycan is an N-linked glycan structure featuring three antennae (branches) extending from the core. These branches often terminate in β-linked galactose (Gal) or N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residues. Triantennary N-glycans are recognized for their high-affinity binding to the rat asialoglycoprotein receptor (AMR). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=The%20efficiency%20of%20the%20AMR%20in,normal%20without%20challenge (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Increased expression of the high-mannose M6N2 and NeuAc3H3N3M3N2F tri-antennary N-glycans in cholangiocarcinoma.[PMID:29399163] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### trimannosyl core

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000175` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:f7cdf4f8-f80e-4ee7-9e4b-9bac207c7b34`

**Definition:**
- A glycan that constitutes the core structure common to all high-mannose, complex, or hybrid N-glycans. The trimannosyl core composition is Man3 GlcNAc2 and includes a chitobiose at the reducing terminal which is extended by a single b-linked Man residue. The b-linked Man is branched by two additional a-linked Man residues, one at the 3 position and one at the 6 position. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- This glycoengineered H. polymorpha strain produced glycoproteins mainly containing trimannosyl core N-glycan (Man(3)GlcNAc(2)), which is the common core backbone of various human-type N-glycans.[PMID:18320567] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### truncated core 1 O-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:65bba6d7-e07f-577c-b95a-b2c2a4b87b0b`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- truncated core 1 structure (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Truncated core 1 O-glycans are O-glycans based on the core 1 structure (Galβ1-3GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr) that are incompletely extended. Examples include the Tn antigen (GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr) and the T antigen (Galβ1-3GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr), which represent early intermediates or incomplete forms of core 1 O-glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Like%20plants%2C%20some%20insect%20cells%20may,core%201%20structures%20%28Tn%20and%20T%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### truncated N-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:cd5c19d8-26c9-5540-b4b5-005c0b9f735c`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Truncated N-glycans are N-linked glycans that are incompletely processed, often due to prolonged retention in the endoplasmic reticulum, resulting in shorter glycan chains than typically found in mature N-glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch39/#:~:text=How%20do%20cells%20distinguish%20between,thought%20to%20be%20devoid%20of%20activity (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### truncated O-GalNAc glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:76a59293-d803-5115-8d91-bfe83bf96e4d`

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- O-glycans initiated by GalNAc on serine or threonine residues that are incompletely extended, including structures such as Tn, STn, and T antigens, which are often associated with cancer. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=In%20addition%2C%20the%20core-fucosylated,residual%20disease%20after%20surgery (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
