### truncated O-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:1a0dea3e-99dc-509f-b1e7-72f22adabe1d`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- incomplete O-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Truncated O-glycans are O-linked glycans that are not fully elongated or processed, often terminating in early core structures such as Tn antigen, T antigen, or their sialylated forms (STn, ST). These incompletely extended glycans frequently expose core structures and are commonly observed in carcinomas, where they can evoke immune responses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Mucins%20might%20also%20interfere%20with,receptor%20%28CAR%29%20T-cell%20therapies (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### type-1 unit

**Term UUID:** `GSD:fe424dbf-51ec-555c-926c-3f41890b54c4`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Galβ1-3GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- type-1 chain repeating unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A disaccharide motif consisting of galactose β1-3 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (Galβ1-3GlcNAc), known as the Type 1 chain. This structure can be repeated, serves as a scaffold for further glycan extension, and acts as a precursor for type 1 H and Lewis antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=Extensions%20by%20Type%201,labile%20under%20alkaline%20conditions (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=The%20Lewis%20b%20antigen%20%28Leb%29%20is,same%20epithelia%20that%20express%20FUT2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### type-2 unit

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b9977dd8-7121-5b87-8b2a-75ca0fbeba71`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Galβ1-4GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- type-2 chain repeating unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- The type-2 unit is a disaccharide motif consisting of galactose β1-4 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (Galβ1-4GlcNAc), which can be repeated and serves as a scaffold for further glycan extension. It acts as a minimal acceptor for glycosyltransferases, is a ligand for galectins, and forms the backbone for the synthesis of blood group H, A, B, and Lewis antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=Extensions%20by%20Type%201,labile%20under%20alkaline%20conditions (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch30/#:~:text=An%20initial%20structure%20for%20the,of%20the%20nucleotide-activated%20Neu5Ac (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch32/#:~:text=Yet%2C%20in%20other%20L-type%20lectins%2C%20no,%C3%85%20%28B%29%20complexed%20with%20Gal%CE%B21-4GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch43/#:~:text=These%20GPI-anchored%20proteins%20form%20a,these%20are%20far%20less%20well%20studied.%20T (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Blood%20group%20i%20and%20I%20antigen,glycolipids%20%28Type-4%29%20%28Figure%2014.4%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- *...and 3 more evidence entries*

### type-4 chain

**Term UUID:** `GSD:8a16aa22-c6c7-504a-b0a9-c4e7a92d84ea`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan chain present in human erythrocyte glycolipids that serves as the backbone for A, B, and H blood group determinants. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Both%20of%20these%20integral%20membrane,on%20Type-1%20units%20%28Figure%2014.4%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### VIM-2

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000180` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G22418PV`

![VIM-2](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G22418PV)

- **Image URL:** [G22418PV](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G22418PV)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G22418PV)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:22a00f8d-9902-4eef-8bc2-2b91bbce1310`

**Definition:**
- α-Neup5Ac-(2→3)-β-D-Galp-(1→4)-β-D-GlcpNAc-(1→3)-β-D-Galp-(1→4)-[α-L-Fucp-(1→3)]-D-GlcpNAc in which the anomeric configuration of the N-acetyl-D-glucosamine residue at the reducing end is β.[CHEBI:85946] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**IUPAC Condensed:**

**Biological Functions:**
- CDw65 have been shown to interact with the ELAM-1 molecule on activated endothelium. (Source: EP0019)

**Disease Associations:**
- chronic myelogenous leukemia. (Source: EP0019)

**Evidence:**
- Anti-SSEA-1 which binds to glycoconjugates with a Gal beta 1-4(fuc alpha 1-3)GlcNAc epitope and VIM-2 which binds to gangliosides with a NeuAc alpha 2-3GlcNAc beta-4(FUC alpha 1-3) GlcNAc beta 1-3Gal-epitope were used to determine the expression of their corresponding carbohydrate antigens in human leukocytes and leukemia cells.[PMID:1690317] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### xyloside

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b301b0d9-2ac9-589a-9cd1-4539be294d96`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Xylosides are glycosides in which xylose serves as the sugar moiety. They are used experimentally to prime the synthesis and secretion of glycosaminoglycan chains in cells. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=Xylosides%20mimic%20the%20natural,disaccharides%20and%20trisaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### xylosylated glycans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:9f46e63f-395f-55cb-9230-c011b593b822`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Xylosylated glycans are glycans that contain xylose residues. These glycans may be attached to glycoproteins or glycolipids and are found in various organisms, including parasites. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch43/#:~:text=The%20LeX%20antigen%2C%20which%20is%20abundant,and%20adaptive%20immune%20responses (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### α-Gal epitope

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000016` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G24432YB`, `G27798EJ`

![α-Gal epitope](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G24432YB)

- **Image URL:** [G24432YB](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G24432YB)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G24432YB)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d7673802-e0d9-5653-b39e-076f90358a60`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- a-Gal antigen (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- αGal (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A glycosylgalactose that consists of β-D-galactose having an α-D-galactosyl residue at the 3-position.[CHEBI:62330] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- The α-Gal epitope is a glycan determinant composed of galactose in an α-linkage. It is notably absent in humans and Old World primates, which results in the production of anti-α-Gal antibodies in these species. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(a1-3)Gal(b1-4)GlcNAc(?1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- Has role as a carbohydrate allergen, epitope or an antigen. (Source: 62330)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch20/#:~:text=Another%20unexplained%20phenomenon%20is,that%20synthesize%20this%20glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Therefore, alpha-gal antigen might be eliminated after a prolonged culture, when using human cells.[PMID:31267683] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### α2-3 sialic acid branch

**Term UUID:** `GSD:7cf55d9d-bacd-51cb-8500-ef798d5c7e9b`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- α2-3Sia branch (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A sialic acid residue linked in an α2-3 configuration that is present as part of a branched glycan structure, such as found on ganglioside GM2. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch15/#:~:text=pneumoniae%29.%20The%20products%20of,have%20not%20been%20well%20investigated (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### β-D-xyloside

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b1e943e8-3b74-5ca0-b624-b7035cb5867d`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycoside in which a β-D-xylose residue is linked to an aglycone. β-D-xylosides can alter proteoglycan biosynthesis by serving as artificial primers for glycosaminoglycan chain initiation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=Xylosides%20mimic%20the%20natural,disaccharides%20and%20trisaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### β-N-acetylgalactosaminide

**Term UUID:** `GSD:ab43ce77-050f-5ca4-aee7-38cc6bd7430d`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan structure featuring an N-acetylgalactosamine residue linked through a beta-glycosidic bond. These structures can serve as primers for oligosaccharide chains on mucins and are known to inhibit O-glycosylation of glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=Xylosides%20mimic%20the%20natural,disaccharides%20and%20trisaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### β-N-acetylglucosaminide

**Term UUID:** `GSD:bbaa2aeb-973c-5aa6-bc50-82c31077387a`

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycan structure containing an N-acetylglucosamine residue linked via a beta-glycosidic bond; it is found as an active glycoside in various oligosaccharides. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=Xylosides%20mimic%20the%20natural,disaccharides%20and%20trisaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### β2-linked N-acetylglucosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:444a9542-bd90-5bca-a8ea-78fc7b905dc4`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- β1-2GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A structural motif where N-acetylglucosamine is linked via a beta-1,2-glycosidic bond, commonly found on the α1-3-mannose arm of N-glycans and known to influence fucosyltransferase substrate specificity. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=Furthermore%2C%20there%20is%20no%20evidence,by%20the%20presence%20of%20%CE%B11-6-mannose (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### β4-linked N-acetylgalactosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:2f37ec04-7fb3-58c1-8f7b-4995e61eda24`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- β1-4GalNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A structural motif in which N-acetylgalactosamine is attached through a beta-1,4-glycosidic bond. This linkage is recognized as an antennal modification on N-glycans, such as those found in sea urchins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch27/#:~:text=However%2C%20its%20receptor%20on%20the%20sperm,%28Figure%2027.2%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### β4-linked N-acetylglucosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:be8acfd3-8241-5bb3-8e7f-21348d2fbb17`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- β1-4GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A structural motif in which N-acetylglucosamine is attached via a beta-1,4-glycosidic bond, typically to mannose on N-glycans, forming the characteristic bisecting GlcNAc feature. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Increased%20expression%20of%20FUT8%20that,depend%20on%20tissue%20context (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### β6-linked N-acetylglucosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:787243fc-eea6-5ac3-b76f-ff0e45698791`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- β1-6GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A β(1-6)-linked N-acetylglucosamine residue that is transferred by core 2 β1-6GlcNAcT enzymes (CGNT1, 3, and 4) during the biosynthesis of core 2 O-GalNAc glycans. This residue often serves as the branching point for poly-LacNAc extensions in mucin glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Similarly%2C%20poly-LacNAc%20extensions,Gal%20engagement%20%28Chapter%2036%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

## Polysaccharides

### 1-4-linked α-L-guluronic acid

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b115b40e-fdf0-573a-9549-0ec68f296584`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- 1-4-linked α-L-GulpA (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- α-L-guluronic acid residues connected through 1-4 glycosidic linkages, forming a structural unit commonly found in alginate polysaccharides produced by brown seaweed. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=These%20polysaccharides%20are%20composed,and%20antitumor%20activities (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### 1-4-linked β-D-mannuronic acid

**Term UUID:** `GSD:28b652e2-5923-5c29-96f1-988d8d23c5e6`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- 1-4-linked β-D-ManpA (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- β-D-mannuronic acid residues connected through 1-4 glycosidic linkages, commonly found as a structural component in alginate polysaccharides produced by brown seaweed. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=These%20polysaccharides%20are%20composed,and%20antitumor%20activities (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### 3-O-sulfated GlcNS6S

**Term UUID:** `GSD:f0e62919-4120-5fd2-af20-33525dfb29a9`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glucosamine residue in heparin or heparan sulfate that is N-sulfated, 6-O-sulfated, and further modified by 3-O-sulfation. This specific modification, known as 3-O-sulfated GlcNS6S, is critical for antithrombin–heparin interaction and is also recognized by certain viruses, highlighting its functional significance in biological processes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=Three%20structural%20elements%20that,diffraction%20to%202.6-%C3%85%20resolution (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch42/#:~:text=In%20many%20cases%2C%20HS%20is%20a%20coreceptor%2C,structure%2C%203-O-sulfation%20on%20GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### 6-O-sulfated glucosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:5f243ab7-64a6-5a23-9ce1-529071201a5a`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Glucosamine residues within heparan sulfate chains that are sulfated at the 6-O position. These residues are subject to modification by endosulfatases (SULFs), which can alter their sulfation status and influence the biological properties of the glycan chain. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch17/#:~:text=An%20epimerase%20%28GLCE%29%2C%20different,cleave%20the%20chains%20at%20limited%20sites (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

---

### amylopectin

**Term UUID:** `GSD:62c19a81-2859-5cac-8aae-c9a9d6da5eb4`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Amylopectin is a branched polysaccharide consisting of α-1,4-linked D-glucose units with α-1,6-linked branch points. It is one of the two major components of starch. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=Amylopectin%20is%20a%20branched%20polymer,walls%20that%20surround%20plant%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### apiogalacturonan

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Apiogalacturonan is a class of plant cell wall pectic polysaccharides characterized by a galacturonic acid backbone with β-D-apiose (Apif) and apiobiose residues attached to the O-2 position of some backbone residues. These polysaccharides are found in duckweeds and seagrasses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### arabinoglucuronoxylan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:687c2e68-ec7d-54ca-a4ee-2996b7df7ebb`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Arabinoglucuronoxylan (AGX) is a type of plant cell wall hemicellulose found in gymnosperms, characterized by a xylan backbone that is substituted with both methylglucuronic acid (MeGlcpA) and arabinofuranose (Araf) residues. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=Glucuronoxylans%2C%20which%20are%20major,their%20reducing%20end%20%28Figure%2024.1C%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### callose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:1083d84a-bf43-50ba-a94d-bf349b194916`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Callose is a plant and fungal polysaccharide composed primarily of β-1,3-linked glucose residues, sometimes featuring β-1,6 branches. It plays important roles in cell wall structure and is involved in defense responses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=Plant%20proteoglycans%20are,of%20N-glycans%20identified%20in%20plants (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Cellulose is a linear polysaccharide made up of β-1,4-linked glucose units. It serves as the primary structural component of plant cell walls and is one of the most abundant biopolymers found in nature. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### chitin

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G97099AY`

![chitin](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G97099AY)

- **Image URL:** [G97099AY](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G97099AY)

- 1-4-linked β-D-GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Chitin is a linear polymer composed of β-D-N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) residues linked via β(1→4) glycosidic bonds. It forms the structural backbone of invertebrate exoskeletons and fungal cell walls, providing strength and rigidity. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### chondroitin

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G43702JT`

![chondroitin](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G43702JT)

- **Image URL:** [G43702JT](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G43702JT)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:c0561c11-1067-5e5a-ba4b-43ad894ccf41`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Chondroitin is a glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units of N-acetylgalactosamine and glucuronic acid. These units can be further modified by sulfation and epimerization. Chondroitin serves as the backbone for chondroitin sulfate and related structures. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch22/#:~:text=Individual%20cells%20of%20Methanosarcina,of%20glucose%20and%20galactose%20acids (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=SQV-4%20and%20SQV-1%20proteins%20represent,the%20eggshell%20and%20the%20embryo (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### chondroitin sulfate

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000043` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:3d250b8c-6c0f-5d52-9fd3-1a58a7660e1d`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Chondroitinsulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- chondroitin polysulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- chondroitin sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- chondroitin sulfate chain (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- chondroitin sulfates (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- chondroitin sulfuric acid (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- chondroitin sulphate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Any of a class of 10‚Äî60 kDa glycosaminoglycan sulfates, widely distributed in cartilage and other mammalian connective tissues.[CHEBI:37397] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Chondroitin sulfate is a sulfated glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units of N-acetylgalactosamine and glucuronic acid, with variable sulfation patterns. It commonly occurs as chains covalently attached to proteoglycans and can be found as oligosaccharide segments (typically 4–20 residues) derived from or mimicking portions of the full polysaccharide. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### chondroitin sulfate A

**Term UUID:** `GSD:0a5b0166-1241-5a4a-a0d8-d7d1970792c6`

**Related Synonyms:**
- chondroitin-4-sulfate (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Chondroitin sulfate A is a subtype of chondroitin sulfate glycosaminoglycan characterized by repeating disaccharide units containing glucuronic acid and N-acetylgalactosamine, with sulfation typically occurring at the 4-position of the N-acetylgalactosamine residue. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### chondroitin sulfate B

**Term UUID:** `GSD:37210836-4c03-58ec-ae10-befc14eacc9e`

**Related Synonyms:**
- dermatan sulfate (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Chondroitin sulfate B, also known as dermatan sulfate, is a glycosaminoglycan characterized by the presence of iduronic acid–containing disaccharide units. It is formed by the epimerization of glucuronic acid in chondroitin sulfate chains. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch17/#:~:text=Heparan%20%28more...%29%20Additional,exolytic%20activities%20%28Chapter%2044%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### dermatan sulfate

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000054` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:0ef0d5e3-6dad-54e6-9bde-79eec7ce6000`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Chondroitin sulfate B (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Dermatan L-iduronate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- beta-Heparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Related Synonyms:**
- chondroitin sulfate B (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Any of a group of glycosaminoglycans with repeating units consisting of variously sulfated β1→4-linked L-iduronyl-(α1→3)-N-acetyl-D-galactosamine units.[CHEBI:18376] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Dermatan sulfate is a glycosaminoglycan (GAG) consisting of repeating disaccharide units of N-acetylgalactosamine and iduronic acid, which is formed by epimerization of glucuronic acid. The chain is modified by O-sulfation and is typically found as a sulfated polysaccharide component of proteoglycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- Wound healing (Source: Chapter 7)
- maintain the structure of the dermis. (Source: Chapter 41)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch17/#:~:text=Vertebrates%20also%20express%20homologs,glucuronic%20acid%20to%20iduronic%20acid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### dextran

### fructans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d5eb4aa0-0881-5a7f-8b55-4027c9e0d7df`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Fructans are storage polysaccharides made primarily of fructose units. They are commonly found in green plants, where they are stored in vacuoles and serve as energy reserves. Fructans may also contribute to plant protection against abiotic stress. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=Amylopectin%20is%20a%20branched%20polymer,walls%20that%20surround%20plant%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### fucoidans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d437d34e-537b-57ce-8704-433177d46d46`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Fucoidans are sulfated polysaccharides mainly composed of fucose residues, typically featuring alternating 1-3 and 1-4 linkages. They are isolated from brown algae and are recognized for their diverse biological activities. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### glycosaminoglycans

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b2e9740e-1fe6-5a6b-97f2-5a4d864900a0`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GAGs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) are linear polysaccharides composed of repeating acidic disaccharide units, typically containing a hexosamine and a hexose or hexuronic acid. These chains are often sulfated and are covalently attached to core proteins to form proteoglycans. GAGs, which include heparan sulfate, chondroitin sulfate, dermatan sulfate, and others, are critical for protein interactions and the structure of the extracellular matrix in eukaryotes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=Thus%2C%20to%20bind%20a%20GAG%20chain%2C%20the,under%20physiological%20conditions (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=Finally%2C%20one%20should%20keep%20in%20mind,inhibitor%20have%20GAG-binding%20sites (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch05/#:~:text=However%2C%20the%20import%20is,of%20misfolded%20glycoproteins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### hemicellulose

**Term UUID:** `GSD:76dafbf0-bbad-51c3-a5ff-fcd0648bef27`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Hemicellulosic polysaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Hemicellulose refers to a class of branched plant cell wall polysaccharides characterized by a backbone of 1-4-linked β-D-pyranosyl residues, such as glucose, mannose, or xylose. This group includes xylans, glucuronoxylans, galactomannans, and xyloglucans. Hemicelluloses are synthesized in the Golgi apparatus and secreted into the apoplast, where they contribute to the structure and function of plant cell walls. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### heparan sulfate

- `GSD000106` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G17927IW`

![heparan sulfate](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G17927IW)

- **Image URL:** [G17927IW](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G17927IW)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G17927IW)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:928fc0f5-5bfb-5be8-9eb6-dd405155d720`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- HS (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Heparan N-sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparan sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparan sulphate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparatan sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparin monosulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparin sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparitin monosulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparitin sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- N-Acetylheparan sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A linear polysaccharide in which the backbone consists of a variably sulfated repeating disaccharide unit.[CHEBI:28815] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Heparan sulfate is a glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units of glucuronic acid (GlcA) and N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc), assembled as a copolymer and extensively modified by sulfation and epimerization. It is synthesized on a linkage tetrasaccharide attached to serine residues of core proteins, forming chains that are found on proteoglycans. Heparan sulfate oligosaccharides are short segments derived from or mimicking these chains. Heparan sulfate plays important roles in cell signaling and molecular interactions. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- Heparan sulfate proteoglycans (HSPGs) play crucial roles regulating key developmental signaling pathways, such as the Wnt, Hedgehog, transforming growth factor-beta, and fibroblast growth factor pathways. (Source:  EP0086)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=A%20main%20technical%20challenge%20to,preference%20of%20GAG-binding%20proteins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### heparin

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000107` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:1b759e1d-9070-5800-9001-ee81290ddc26`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Cy 222 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Heparinic acid (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- bemiparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- certoparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- enoxaparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- fluxum (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- parnaparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- reviparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- sandoparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A highly sulfated linear glycosaminoglycan comprising complex patterns of uronic acid-(1→4)-D-glucosamine repeating subunits. Used as an injectable anticoagulant, it has the highest negative charge density of any known biological molecule.[CHEBI:28304] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Heparin is a highly sulfated glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units, typically containing N-sulfated glucosamine and iduronic acid, both variably sulfated. Structurally related to heparan sulfate, heparin chains are often attached to core proteins and are well known for their anticoagulant activity and high affinity for various proteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

- Heteroxylan refers to a class of hemicellulosic polysaccharides found in the secondary cell walls of seed-producing plants. ### hyaluronan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000183` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G29086KO`

![hyaluronan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G29086KO)

- **Image URL:** [G29086KO](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G29086KO)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G29086KO)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:6811049f-c1fb-5111-bcf3-360ba4f8e672`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- GlcAβ1-3GlcNAcβ1-4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- HA (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Hyaluronan is a high-molecular-weight, linear, nonsulfated glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units of glucuronic acid (GlcA) β1-3 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) β1-4. These long, polyanionic chains occupy large hydrodynamic volumes and play important roles in cell adhesion, signaling, and tissue structure. Short hyaluronan chains or oligosaccharides, generated by enzymatic cleavage, can interact with membrane proteins such as CD44 and TLR2/4, modulating signaling pathways and inducing biological effects including apoptosis in cancer cells. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Members%20of%20the%20CEACAM%20family%20have,tumor%20cell%20survival%20and%20invasion (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch16/#:~:text=Hyaluronan%20has%20an%20indefinite%20and,partially%20or%20completely%20excluded (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch16/#:~:text=Thus%2C%20the%20presence%20of%20the%20BX7B,signaling%20in%20health%20and%20disease (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch16/#:~:text=Thus%2C%20the%20formation%20of%20hyaluronan,for%20a%20number%20of%20years (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=To%20prevent%20excessive%20bleeding%2C,cell%20adhesion%20%28see%20Chapter%2015%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

![keratan sulfate](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G82109MW)

- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G82109MW)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:55518b88-605c-5d07-a328-555ccbea84d0`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- KS chain (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Keratan 6'-sulphate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Keratan sulphate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Kerato 6'-sulphate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Kerato sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)
- Kerato sulphate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

- kerato 6'-sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**

**Definition:**
- A sulfated glycosaminoglycan, a linear polymer that consists of the repeating disaccharide [3)-β-Gal-(1→4)-β-GlcNAc-(1→] and containing sulfo groups located at random positions.[CHEBI:60924] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Keratan sulfate (KS) is a glycosaminoglycan (GAG) composed of repeating disaccharide units of galactose and N-acetylglucosamine, which are variably sulfated. These sulfated poly-N-acetyllactosamine chains are typically attached to core proteins at asparagine or serine/threonine residues, forming proteoglycans such as decorin, biglycan, versican, and lumican in various tissues. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- Tissue hydration. (Source:  11030741)

### Leishmania lipophosphoglycan repeat unit

**Term UUID:** `GSD:898a8243-636a-5c97-90ac-fa638de41d03`

**Exact Synonyms:**

- Galβ1-4Manα1-PO4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A repeating disaccharide-phosphate unit composed of galactose (β1-4 linked to mannose) with a phosphate group, forming the backbone of the phosphoglycan polymer in Leishmania lipophosphoglycan (LPG) and related phosphoglycosylated protein glycans. This structural motif is characteristic of the phosphoglycan portions of LPG and proteophosphoglycan (PPG) in certain protozoa. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Lipoteichoic acids (LTAs) are anionic glycopolymers present in the cell envelopes of Gram-positive bacteria. They typically consist of polyglycerol phosphate chains anchored to a glycolipid, and display diverse carbohydrate structures. Their biosynthetic pathways are distinct from those of wall teichoic acids. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- Mannosylated lipoarabinomannan (ManLAM) is a form of lipoarabinomannan (LAM) found in mycobacteria, characterized by mannose residues capping its structure. As a glycolipid, ManLAM plays a key role in modulating immune responses by interacting with C-type lectin receptors. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### poly-N-acetyllactosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:6b4d591f-cc04-56e8-a565-f8198ae070e4`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Galβ1-4GlcNAcβ1-3 repeats (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- poly-LN (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- poly-LacNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- poly-N-acetyllactosamine sequences (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Poly-N-acetyllactosamine (poly-LN) refers to a linear or branched glycan motif composed of repeating disaccharide units of galactose β1-4 linked to N-acetylglucosamine β1-3, denoted as (Galβ1-4GlcNAcβ1-3)n. These chains are commonly found on N- and O-glycans as well as glycolipids, where they can be further modified with sialic acid and fucose. Poly-N-acetyllactosamine structures serve as scaffolds for terminal glycan modifications and are recognized by galectins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**

**Term UUID:** `GSD:68aeaab4-aaf3-5255-adab-08c0a0d5453d`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- Polysaccharide A (PSA) is a capsular polysaccharide characterized by zwitterionic properties. It is notable for its immunomodulatory role and its involvement in the maturation of the immune system. Polysaccharide A is distinct from polysialic acids. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### polysialic acid

**GSD IDs:**

- polySia (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Polysialic acid (polySia) is a linear homopolymer of sialic acid (N-acetylneuraminic acid, Neu5Ac) residues, typically linked via α2-8 or α2-9 glycosidic bonds. - Pseudomurein is a glycan-based polymer present in the cell walls of certain methanogenic Archaea. It consists of glycan strands cross-linked by peptides composed of L-amino acids, and is structurally distinct from the peptidoglycan found in bacterial cell walls. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- Rhamnogalacturonan I (RG-I) is a pectic polysaccharide characterized by a backbone of repeating disaccharide units composed of 4-α-D-galacturonic acid (GalpA) and 2-α-L-rhamnose (Rhap). **Description:**
- Sulfated glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) are a class of linear polysaccharides that include heparan sulfate, chondroitin sulfate, dermatan sulfate, and keratan sulfate. They play important roles in cell signaling, extracellular matrix structure, and protein interactions. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### xyloglucan

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G25326UC`

![xyloglucan](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G25326UC)

- **Image URL:** [G25326UC](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G25326UC)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G25326UC)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:6e9249d9-1b1b-5dbf-8561-76de16821a00`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Xyloglucan is a major hemicellulosic polysaccharide found in plant cell walls, consisting of a β-1,4-glucan backbone substituted with α-D-xylose and sometimes other sugars. It plays a key role in cell wall structure and expansion by acting as a tether between cellulose microfibrils. Oligosaccharide fragments derived from the enzymatic or chemical breakdown of xyloglucan can trigger defense responses in plants and provide protection against pathogens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch40/#:~:text=Xyloglucan%20oligosaccharides,function%20as%20signaling%20molecules (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch59/#:~:text=Current%20research%20is%20focused%20on,125%20years%20%28the%20viscose%20process%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch08/#:~:text=Free-living%20fungi%20and%20the,the%20action%20of%20dozens%20of%20GTs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### β-(1,3)-glucan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:602bc4bf-da9f-505c-8a5d-d3de8020b72d`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- β-1,3-glucan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- β-1,3-glucan oligosaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- β-(1,3)-glucan is a polysaccharide composed of glucose units linked by β-(1,3) glycosidic bonds. It can be linear or branched and is a major structural component of fungal and oomycete cell walls. β-(1,3)-glucan can also be released as oligosaccharides, which may function as pathogen-associated molecular patterns (PAMPs) in plants. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=GPI%20biosynthesis%20and%20transfer%20to,from%20detection%20by%20macrophages (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### β-(1,4)-glucan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:3f1f513f-f9df-5475-8bb2-ca5235e3de00`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- β-1,4-glucan oligosaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- β-1,4-glucans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A polysaccharide composed of glucose residues linked by β-1,4-glycosidic bonds; it is the main structural component of cellulose in plant cell walls. Oligosaccharides released from cellulose can act as signaling molecules in plants. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch40/#:~:text=More%20recently%2C%20additional%20plant,fungal%20and%20oomycete%20cell%20walls (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch40/#:~:text=For%20example%2C%20%CE%B2-1%2C3-glucan,respectively%20%28Figure%2040.2%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### β-(1,4)-mannuronate

**Term UUID:** `GSD:e8b1df23-5a49-5fd5-ba6a-9e4701e140d1`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A polysaccharide or oligosaccharide unit composed of mannuronic acid residues linked by β-1,4-glycosidic bonds; these structures form part of alginate, a major component of some bacterial and algal extracellular matrices. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch53/#:~:text=%28A%29%20Schematic%20overview%20of,strong%20base%20and%20hydrogenolysis (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### β-glucan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:6c978e40-5b00-580b-b7c6-94bc0e970ccc`

**Classification:**
- Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- β-glucan refers to a class of polysaccharides made up of glucose monomers connected mainly by β-glycosidic bonds. These molecules are commonly found in the cell walls of fungi and plants, and they can exhibit structural diversity, including linear or mixed-linkage forms such as β-1,3 and β-1,4 linkages. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=It%20is%20clear%20that%20purified%20GPI-APs,by%20the%20actions%20of%20Dfg5%20and%20Dcw1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch23/#:~:text=To%20convey%20the%20enormous%20diversity,plants%20and%20algae%20than%20in%20fungi.%20D (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=A%20small%20number%20of%20the%20backbone,produced%20by%20plants%20%28Figure%2024.1G%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=MBL%2C%20conglutinin%2C%20CL-43%2C%20CL-46%2C,is%20further%20augmented%20by%20SP-A (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=MINCLE%20also%20maintains,yeast%20and%20fungal%20infections (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A fucosylated glycan with two sialic acid components. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### fucosylated disialylated biantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000074` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:866054d3-8ca5-45cc-b742-d8bd1bd25a03`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- fucosylated disialo-biantennary (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Definition:**
- Describing a biantennary oligosaccharide that has two sialic acid component and fucose added. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- We found that approximately 63% of the total N-linked oligosaccharides were of the complex type, with bisialo-biantennary (28%), asialo-biantennary (7%), fucosylated bisialo-biantennary (25%), and fucosylated asialo-biantennary (3%) oligosaccharides representing the major complex-type carbohydrate species.[PMID:2065054] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 1 references
- [PMID:2065054](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/2065054/)

### fucosylated trisialylated triantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000172` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:569eebe7-730f-4e21-8029-7101bad0fbe9`

**Definition:**
- A triantennary glycan with three GlcNAc branches linked to the core and the addition of three sialic acid residues and fucose added. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- Diantennary monosialylated glycans and triantennary trisialylated fucosylated and non-fucosylated structures have also been identified.[PMID:17591618] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 1 references
- [PMID:17591618](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/17591618/)

- Neu5Acα2-3Gal is a disaccharide motif where N-acetylneuraminic acid (Neu5Ac) is linked in an alpha 2-3 configuration to galactose (Gal). This structure is commonly found as a terminal motif on glycans and is recognized by certain viruses and lectins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### nonsialylated N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000136` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:b557a57f-2ea1-404a-a7f1-8ef061e6ff1b`

**Definition:**
- A glycan which is not sialylated. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- The pattern of nonsialylated oligosaccharides was used for interpretation of the fully sialylated species using bioinformatics tools. From pooled human plasma, we find 90, 101, and 64 different glycan compositions for genetic variants ORM1*F1, ORM1*S, and ORM2, respectively. Glycan structures carry dominantly between 15 and 16 sialic acids indicating an almost complete termination of all antenae with sialic acid.[PMID:30295034] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### sialyl-Tn

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000157` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G36123IU`

![sialyl-Tn](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G36123IU)

- **Image URL:** [G36123IU](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G36123IU)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G36123IU)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:562a7a4f-9d8e-5682-ab69-9673ba3599c6`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- Neu5Acα2-6GalNAcα (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- sTn antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- sialyl-Tn antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- N-acetyl-alpha-neuraminyl-(2→6)-N-acetyl-alpha-D-galactosamine is an N-acetyl-alpha-neuraminyl-(2→6)-N-acetyl-D-galactosamine in which the carbon bearing the anomeric hydroxy group has alpha configuration. It has a role as an epitope. It derives from a N-acetyl-alpha-D-galactosamine and a N-acetyl-alpha-neuraminic acid.[CHEBI:61818] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- The Sialyl-Tn antigen is a glycan structure composed of N-acetylneuraminic acid (Neu5Ac) linked in an α2-6 configuration to N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) in α configuration. It is recognized as a tumor-associated carbohydrate antigen. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Neu5Ac(a2-6)GalNAc(a1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- apoptosis of monocyte-derived dendritic cells (Source: EP0022)
- decrease of adhesion and increase of migration (Source: EP0022)
- increase of tumor growth (Source: EP0022)
- associated with morphological changes, decreased growth and increased migration of MDA-MB-231 cells. (Source: EP0022)

**Disease Associations:**
- anaplastic meningioma (Source: EP0022)
- Barrett's esophagus (Source: EP0022)
- bladder cancer (Source: EP0022)
- breast cancer (Source: EP0022)
- colon cancer (Source: EP0022)
- colorectal cancer (Source: EP0022)
- erythroblastic leukemia (Source: EP0022)
- esophageal cancer (Source: EP0022)
- gastric cancer (Source: EP0022)
- helicobacter pylori chronic gastritis(HpCG) (Source: EP0022)
- liver cancer (Source: EP0022)
- iterome cancer (Source: EP0022)
- ovary tumor (Source: EP0022)
- pancreas cancer (Source: EP0022)

### sialyl-Tn antigen

**Term UUID:** `GSD:56c1c4e3-2f6b-59db-b7c5-46e6567feea0`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- sTn (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Sialyl-Tn is a truncated O-glycan structure (Neu5Acα2-6GalNAcα-) formed by the sialylation of the Tn antigen. It is commonly overexpressed in various cancers and serves as a notable cancer-associated glycan epitope. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Cell%20lines%20help%20to%20answer%20certain,%28Tn%2C%20sialyl-Tn%29%20in%20cancer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=Currently%2C%20several%20new%20vaccines,failed%20to%20gain%20marketing%20approval (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch35/#:~:text=These%20findings%20suggest%20that,motif%20in%20its%20cytoplasmic%20tail (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### sialylated biantennary N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000154` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:2af27ed8-674b-5471-adb0-e8456d8f0ea1`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- sialyl biantennary (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- A binantennary glycan with one or two sialic acid residues as non-reducing terminal residues on one or both antennae. [RN] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- A type of N-linked glycan characterized by a core structure with two antennae (biantennary), each ending with sialic acid residues. These glycans are commonly found on human glycoproteins, including therapeutic proteins such as erythropoietin. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Human%20core%201%20O-glycan%20biosynthesis,S2%20Drosophila%20melanogaster%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Simbucus nigra agglutinin or an antibody specific to sialylated lactosamine with a preference for Neu5Acalpha2-6Gal rather than Neu5Acalpha2-3Gal reduced sperm binding to oviduct isthmic cells, as did occupying putative receptors on sperm with sialylated biantennary glycans.[PMID:23115267] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### sialylated complex-type N-glycan

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000155` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:13c3b217-f4d2-5e5f-9349-c2754539e341`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- complex sialylated N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- complex-type sialylated glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- sialo-complex type (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Complex N-linked glycan that have added GlcNAc residues at both the α-3 and α-6 mannose sites and do not contain mannose residues apart from the core structure but with sialic acid residues. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- Complex-type N-glycans that are modified by the addition of sialic acid residues at the terminal positions of their branches. These sialylated structures contribute to a negative charge, play roles in biological recognition, and are often used to enhance the circulatory half-life and alter the biodistribution of glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Biological Functions:**
- enhance the signaling activity of soluble intercellular adhesion molecule-1 in mouse astrocytes [PMID: 15201278] Appears in IgG-Fc. (Source: 20647032 )

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=The%20baculovirus-insect%20cell,add%20terminal%20sialic%20acids (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Combining%20knockout%20of%20St3gal4%20and,and%20improved%20biodistribution (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- By using 1H-NMR spectroscopy we were able to establish that our product contains a single N-linked biantennary , fully sialylated complex-type oligosaccharide , typical of human hepatomas.[PMID:1694115] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 6 references
- [PMID:11058758](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11058758/)
- [PMID:15201278](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15201278/)
- [PMID:1694115](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1694115/)
- [PMID:28342150](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28342150/)
- [PMID:30471292](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30471292/)
- [PMID:30969765](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30969765/)

### sialylated glycolipid

**Term UUID:** `GSD:35ef481e-71f9-5f52-8643-a36b9c465ab7`

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Glycolipids that contain sialic acid residues, which are recognized by myelin-associated glycoprotein and play a role in the inhibition of neuronal sprouting. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### sialylated glycosphingolipid

**Classification:**
- Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Sialylated glycosphingolipids are glycosphingolipids that contain sialic acid residues. They are widely distributed in animal families, with the exception of arthropods, and are also referred to as gangliosides. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### sialylated N-acetyllactosamine

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d6d5d9b1-e690-541d-b5fb-6b58d336553a`

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**

### sialylated N-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:66cd1dd7-b3bf-5df6-b3c3-69e6354149e1`

**Classification:**
- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Sialylated N-glycans are N-linked glycans that possess terminal sialic acid residues. These structures are commonly found on glycoproteins, such as the IgG Fc domain, and play important roles in modulating antibody effector functions and other biological processes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch41/#:~:text=Glycans%20are%20critical%20components%20of,underlying%20endothelial%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### sialylated O-GalNAc glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:2f79f606-6a5c-5ac0-ae1f-b931f02464ef`

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- O-linked glycans that are initiated by N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) and further modified by the addition of sialic acid residues, typically through the action of α2-3 sialyltransferases such as ST3GAL1. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=A%20family%20of%20%CE%B12-6,Gal%20residue%20of%20core%201%20O-glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### sialylated O-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:846e7a18-60cf-5ee0-bc8a-337519077430`

**Classification:**
- O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Sialylated O-glycans are O-linked glycans that possess terminal sialic acid residues. These structures can be biosynthesized in engineered yeast expressing human sialyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Examples%20of%20this%20include%20the%20hinge,O-glycan%20engineering%20in%20yeast (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:8e044e80-03e0-433f-84b8-89c3b621e02a`

### tetrasialylated biantennary N-glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:bcdcd7e0-2593-46c4-bd21-9ef67fdaaa50`

### α-sialyl linkage

**Term UUID:** `GSD:074c18ce-d0db-5638-b2f1-18f7c5525a09`

- α-sialyl linkages (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**

- A glycosidic linkage where sialic acid is attached to another sugar through an alpha configuration. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

- α2-3sialyllactosamines (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A disaccharide motif consisting of sialic acid linked α2-3 to lactosamine (Galβ1-4GlcNAc), commonly found in glycoproteins and glycolipids, and produced by group B streptococci. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### ceramide dihexosyl sulfate

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000041` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G04416BG`

![ceramide dihexosyl sulfate](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G04416BG)

- **Image URL:** [G04416BG](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G04416BG)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G04416BG)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:054bf2b0-7e12-4db2-ad82-3f65435eeb78`

**Definition:**
- A sulfatide with two hexose moieties (glucose and galactose) has been isolated from human kidney (Martensson E, Acta Chem Scand 1963, 17, 1174). Equimolar amounts of ceramide, glucose, galactose, and sulfate are found in the molecule. The sulfate group is esterified, as in sulfatides, with carbon 3 of the galactose moiety. The linkage between galactose and glucose is identical to lactose (b-1-4). Its fatty acid composition is similar to that of sulfatides. [http://cyberlipid.gerli.com/description/complex-lipids/glycolipids/glycosphingolipids/sulfosphingolipids/] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Evidence:**
- Although it has been reported that ceramide dihexosyl sulfate is present in human kidney, it is unlikely that it is present in equine kidney.[PMID:818074] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 1 references
- [PMID:818074](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/818074/)

- N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A type of N-glycan that carries corneal-type keratan sulfate (KS, type I) chains and is degraded by enzymes involved in N-glycan catabolism. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch44/#:~:text=Galactose-6-SO4%20sulfatase%20is%20the,sulfatase%20deficiency%20%28MSD%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:bf54b03e-d24b-5886-997b-2a52ede11173`

### sulfated glycan

**Term UUID:** `GSD:a2613919-1e6d-5428-aba4-5997bad45f8b`

**Classification:**
- Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Sulfated glycans are glycans that contain sulfate groups. This category includes a variety of structures, such as those recognized by L-selectin, the HNK-1 epitope, and pituitary glycoprotein hormones. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=However%2C%20in%20vertebrates%2C%20glycan,is%20remarkably%20rare%20in%20prokaryotes (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### sulfated terminal β-linked GalNAc

**Term UUID:** `GSD:e0e0fe41-e988-5381-a1a6-932e18899850`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- sulfated-GalNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- Terminal N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residues that are β-linked and sulfated. These motifs are found on pituitary glycoprotein hormones such as lutropin (LH) and thyrotropin (TSH), where they play a role in the structure and function of these glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=The%20Forssman%20antigen%2C%20first,it%20is%20made%20in%20the%20same%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Consequently%2C%20the%20biantennary,of%20the%20hormone%20by%20the%20pituitary (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

## Tumor-Associated Antigens

### I antigen

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000112` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G56045WU`

![I antigen](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G56045WU)

- **Image URL:** [G56045WU](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G56045WU)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G56045WU)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:d09ee5db-281c-40b1-95c3-53f11f4aef41`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- branched poly-n-acetyllactosamine units (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- N-[(2R,3R,4R,5S,6R)-5-[(2S,3R,4S,5S,6R)-4-[(2R,3R,4R,5S,6R)-3-Acetamido-4-hydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)-5-[(2S,3R,4S,5R,6R)-3,4,5-trihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-2-yl]oxyoxan-2-yl]oxy-6-[[(2R,3R,4R,5S,6R)-3-acetamido-4-hydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)-5-[(2S,3R,4S,5R,6R)-3,4,5-trihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-2-yl]oxyoxan-2-yl]oxymethyl]-3,5-dihydroxyoxan-2-yl]oxy-2,4-dihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-3-yl]acetamide (CHEBI:151390) is a amino sugar.[CHEBI:151390] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- A branched poly-N-acetyllactosamine glycan determinant formed by β1-6 branching of repeating Galβ1-4GlcNAc units, distinguishing it from the linear i antigen. It is carried on N- and O-glycans and glycolipids, serves as part of the I blood group system, and its expression changes during development (i → I conversion) and in certain diseases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(b1-4)GlcNAc(b1-3)[Gal(b1-4)GlcNAc(b1-6)]Gal(b1-4)GlcNAc(b1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=However%2C%20core%203%20and%204%20O-GalNAc,sugars%20or%20functional%20groups (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- The I antigen is formed by the action of an enzyme (a glycosyltransferase), which adds branches onto the i antigen.[PMID:15679458] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Publications:** 8 references

### i antigen

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000111` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G00058MO`, `G46055MA`

![i antigen](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G00058MO)

**Description:**
- The i antigen consists of repeating disaccharide units of N-acetyllactosamine (Galβ1-4GlcNAcβ1-3/6) arranged in a linear fashion. These linear poly-N-acetyllactosamine chains can act as precursors for further branching and are involved in the biosynthesis of antigens such as the I antigen. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**

- Inasmuch as the hybridoma was established by hybridization of lymphocytes derived from regional lymph nodes of lung cancer, and the antigen was found in the patient's lung cancer tissue, the i antigen in lung cancer is probably recognized as a tumor-associated antigen by the host's immune cell system.[PMID:2422274] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### O-antigen

**Term UUID:** `GSD:831262e4-f719-5510-a84a-ee900c77b375`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- LPS O antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- O-PS (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- O-polysaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- lipopolysaccharide O antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- The O-antigen is a repetitive glycan polymer that constitutes the outermost domain of the lipopolysaccharide (LPS) in Gram-negative bacteria. It is highly variable in structure and serves as a major determinant of antigenic diversity among bacterial strains. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch05/#:~:text=In%20%E2%80%9Clower%E2%80%9D%20eukaryotes%20and,in%20eukaryotic%20cells%20%28Chapter%209%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch03/#:~:text=LPSs%20carry%20long%20polysaccharides,diverse%20set%20of%20polymer%20structures (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch03/#:~:text=The%20polysaccharide%20may%20also%20be,and%20rhamsan%20%28S-194%29%20%28Figure%203.4F%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch03/#:~:text=Some%20polymers%20are%20known%20under,affect%20polysaccharide%20properties (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch05/#:~:text=Deoxyhexoses%20are%20often,CDP-glucose%20dehydratase (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- *...and 5 more evidence entries*

### P antigen

**Term UUID:** `GSD:cf106ff8-f5cf-494a-a7bd-47029cde0d2b`

**Related Synonyms:**
- Gb4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- P1PK Blood Group System (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Description:**
- A glycosphingolipid antigen with the structure GalNAcβ1-3Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer, belonging to the globoseries. It is the defining determinant of the P blood group system, carried on glycolipids of red blood cells and other tissues. Along with related glycans such as Gb3 (Pk antigen), P antigen can act as a receptor for microbial toxins and lectins, and has been implicated in cancer cell recognition and signaling. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Evidence:**
- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=Interaction%20between%20prototype,Pk%20and%20P%20antigens%20%28Chapter%2014%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

### P1 antigen

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000141` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G12460DL`, `G55205ZV`

![P1 antigen](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G12460DL)

- **Image URL:** [G12460DL](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G12460DL)

**Exact Synonyms:**
- P1 determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- An amino pentasaccharide consisting of α-D-galactose, β-D-galactose N-acetyl-α-D-glucosamine, β-D-galactose, and β-D-glucose residues joined in sequence with ((1→4)-, (1→4)-, (1→3)- and (1→4)-linkages, respectively.[CHEBI:68484] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- The P1 antigen is a specific glycan structure expressed on the urothelium, where it facilitates bacterial attachment and infection. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(a1-4)Gal(b1-4)GlcNAc(?1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

- The P1 antigen was disovered in 1927 by Landsteiner and Levine, and Pk and NOR were described in 1951 and 1982, respectively.[PMID: 24046920] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### Sda antigen

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000145` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

- A branched amino tetrasaccharide comprised of a trisaccharide chain of N-acetyl-α-neuraminic acid, β-D-galactose and N-acetyl-β-D-glucosamine residues linked sequentially (2→3) and (1→4), to the galactose residue of which is also linked (1→4) an N-acetyl-β-L-galactosamine residue. - An amino disaccharide composed of D-galactose and N-acetyl-α-D-galactosaminyl residues in β-(1→3) linkage.[CHEBI:61820] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- The T antigen is a disaccharide glycan structure consisting of galactose (Gal) β1-3 linked to N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) O-linked to serine or threonine, representing the core 1 structure of mucin-type O-glycans. It is commonly found as a core 1 O-glycan on glycoproteins, recognized by certain lectins, and is associated with tumor cells. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(a1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- accumulation Lipoprotein(a) or LDL within arterial walls in atherogenesis (Source: EP0020)
- cancer progression (increase interaction/communication of the cells) (Source: EP0020)
- T antigen expression in RMA lymphoma cells leads to a reduced growth of the tumor (Source: EP0020)
- correlation with elevated Golgi pH in cancer cell (breast, colorectal cancer cells) (Source: EP0020)
- T antigen on apical surfaces of corneal epithelial cells provide protection against rose bengal penetrance. (Source: EP0020)
- reduction of a flexibility of the glycosidic bond and thereby favour certain glycan conformation (Source: EP0020)
- T antigen is a target for NK cell-mediated lysis (Source: EP0020)
- inverse relationship between T antigen expression and VWF plasma levels (Source: EP0020)

**Disease Associations:**
- anaplastic meningioma (Source: EP0020)
- breast cancer (Source: EP0020)
- colorectal cancer (Source: EP0020)
- colon cancer (Source: EP0020)
- esophageal cancer (Source: unknown)
- gastric cancer (Source: EP0020)
- liver cancer (Source: EP0020)
- ovarian cancer (Source: EP0020)
- prostate cancer (Source: EP0020)
- bladder tumor (Source: EP0020)
- invasive pneumococcal infection (hemolytic uremic syndrome, hemolytic anemia) (Source: EP0020)

**Evidence:**

- https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=R-type%20lectins%20are%20present%20in,of%20cultured%20mammalian%20tumor%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Tn, sTn, and T antigen neo- or over-expression occurs in many types of cancer including gastric, colon, breast, lung, esophageal, prostate, and endometrial cancer.[PMID:27679419] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

### Tn antigen

**GSD IDs:**
- `GSD000167` (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**GlyTouCan IDs:** `G57321FI`

![Tn antigen](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G57321FI)

- **Image URL:** [G57321FI](https://api.glycosmos.org/wurcs2image/latest/png/binary/G57321FI)
- **GlyTouCan:** [View Structure](https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G57321FI)

**Term UUID:** `GSD:25e2c658-4349-5dc1-ba23-f9dbe366f1e7`

**Exact Synonyms:**
- O-GalNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)
- Thomsen-Nouveau antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Classification:**
- Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**Definition:**
- Tn anitigen or alpha-galnac or N-Acetylgalactosamine is an N-acetyl-D-galactosamine having alpha-configuration at the anomeric centre. It has a role as an epitope. It derives from an alpha-D-galactosamine.[CHEBI:40356,HMDB0000212] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0)

**Description:**
- The Tn antigen is a single N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residue O-linked to serine or threonine on proteins. It represents the simplest O-GalNAc glycan structure and is the initial product of mucin-type O-glycosylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E)

**IUPAC Condensed:**
- `GalNAc(a1-` (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE)

**Biological Functions:**
- lymphatic metastasis (Source: EP0021)
- tube formation in Drosophila development (Source: EP0021)
- modulation of malignant phenotype (gastric carcinoma cell) (Source: EP0021)
- correlation with tumor stage, lymphatic invasion and lymph node matastasis (Source: EP0021)

**Disease Associations:**
- breast cancer (Source: EP0021)

## 📚 About This Dictionary

This glycan structure dictionary was automatically generated from the GSD (Glycan Structure Dictionary) JSON database. All structure images are rendered in SNFG (Symbol Nomenclature for Glycans) format using the [GlyCosmos API](https://glycosmos.org/).

### Data Sources

This dictionary combines information from multiple authoritative sources:

- **GSD_GLYGEN_V0**: GlyGen Glycan Structure Database
- **EOG_VARKI_4E**: Essentials of Glycobiology (4th Edition)
- **PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE**: PubDictionaries Glycan Image Database
- **Additional sources**: Various specialized glycan databases

Each entry consolidates information from multiple sources, with source attribution provided for each piece of information.

### Image Format

All images use the **SNFG** (Symbol Nomenclature for Glycans) notation:
- 🔵 **Blue circle:** Glucose (Glc)
- 🟡 **Yellow circle:** Galactose (Gal)
- 🟢 **Green circle:** Mannose (Man)
- 🟣 **Purple diamond:** N-Acetylneuraminic acid (Neu5Ac)
- 🟨 **Yellow square:** N-Acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc)
- 🔷 **Blue square:** N-Acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc)
- 🔺 **Red triangle:** Fucose (Fuc)
